# Wolvenkit.CLI: Usage | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage#summary)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

This page contains information on the general use of Wolvenkit's command line interface. For more specific information, please see the corresponding sub-pages:

*   [Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage/settings)
    
*   [Wolvenkit.CLI: Command List](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage/command-list)
    
*   [Textures (CLI)](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage/textures-cli)
    
*   [Video and audio files (CLI)](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage/video-and-audio-files-cli)
    

Help[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage#help)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Display general help/list all commands: `--help`
    
*   Display help for a specific command: `archive -h`
    

Using WolvenKit Console globally[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage#using-wolvenkit-console-globally)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can now install and update the Wolvenkit Command Line Tools (wolvenkit.cli) as a _dotnet global tool_. The console can be used anywhere directly from the command line, with no custom installation, and easy update.

Open command prompt/powershell and type in:

`dotnet tool install -g wolvenkit.cli`

WolvenKit Console can now be run from anywhere using CMD/Powershell:

`wolvenkit.cli uncook -p "D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Cyberpunk 2077\archive\pc\content\basegame_1_engine.archive"`

For updates to WolvenKit console, open CMD/Powershell and type:

`dotnet tool update --global wolvenkit.cli`

Video Guide for launching CLI[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage#video-guide-for-launching-cli)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Processing files in bulk[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/usage#processing-files-in-bulk)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To process multiple .archive files (for e.g. extracting duplicate textures), write yourself a batch file:

[PreviousWolvenKit Console](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-cli/wolvenkit-console)

Was this helpful?

Copy

    @echo off
    setlocal
    
    set "cli_path=C:\01_apps\Wolvenkit_CLI_8_15\Wolvenkit.CLI.exe"
    set "modpath=F:\CyberpunkFiles\temp"
    
    FOR %%F IN ("%modpath%\*.archive") DO (
        SET "baseName=%%~nF"
        "%cli_path%" uncook -p "%%F" -o "%modpath%\output"  REM -w *.xbm --uext png
        
        REM Move PNG files and preserve relative paths
        FOR /R "%modpath%\output" %%G IN (*.png) DO (
            SET "relPath=%%~pG"
            SETLOCAL enabledelayedexpansion
            SET "newDir=%modpath%\out\!baseName!\!relPath!"
            mkdir "!newDir!" >nul 2>&1
            move "%%G" "!newDir!"
            ENDLOCAL
        )
    )
    
    PAUSE
    EXIT

# FAQ | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq.md)
.

What can I do with WolvenKit?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq#what-can-i-do-with-wolvenkit)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The [**Overview**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview)
 page provides a broad look at what's possible to achieve with WolvenKit.

How do I download WolvenKit?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq#how-do-i-download-wolvenkit)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Follow the instructions on the [**Download page**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/download)
.

Where can I get help with WolvenKit?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq#where-can-i-get-help-with-wolvenkit)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Browsing this wiki is the best place to start! Follow the Getting Started pages, especially the guide for [**Creating a Mod**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/creating-a-mod)
. For additional help from other WolvenKit users, check out our [**Community**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/community)
 information.

How can I open or browse archive files?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq#how-can-i-open-or-browse-archive-files)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is capable of directly reading the .archive format. Archives do not need to be dumped or exported for use. The Asset Browser is capable of browsing any archive with instant previews for assets such as textures and models. Learn more on the [**dedicated Asset Browser page**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/asset-browser)
. Do you need help learning how to use WolvenKit? Try following the guide for [**Creating a Mod**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/creating-a-mod)
!

How can I import or export textures with WolvenKit?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq#how-can-i-import-or-export-textures-with-wolvenkit)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is capable of exporting textures to common formats such as TGA, DDS, PNG, JPG, etc. Additionally formats such as TGA and DDS are supported for importing textures. Check the [**dedicated Textures page**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/import-export/textures)
 to learn more. Do you need help learning how to use WolvenKit? Try following the guide for [**Creating a Mod**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/creating-a-mod)
!

How can I import or export models with WolvenKit?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/faq#how-can-i-import-or-export-models-with-wolvenkit)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is capable of exporting models natively to glTF, and other formats such as FBX through conversion. Additionally the glTF format is supported for importing models. Check the [**dedicated Models page**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/import-export/models)
 to learn more. Do you need help learning how to use WolvenKit? Try following the guide for [**Creating a Mod**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/creating-a-mod)
!

[PreviousCommunity](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/community)

# Core Mods explained | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained.md)
.

In the context of Cyberpunk modding, the core mods are often interchangeably called "(core) frameworks".

If you are trying to troubleshoot your core mods, you can run the [log file utility](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/user-guide-troubleshooting/finding-and-reading-log-files#finding-the-log-files)
.

TL;DR: What do I download?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#tl-dr-what-do-i-download)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For an explanation of what the individual core mods do, see below.

[Redscript](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/1511)

[RED4ext](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/2380)

[Cyber Engine Tweaks](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/107)

[ArchiveXL](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/4198)
 – [TweakXL](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/4197)
 – [Codeware](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/7780)
 – [Equipment-EX](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/6945)

What is a core mod?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#what-is-a-core-mod)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

While the game natively supports mods, this would have had us limited to replacing already existing items, but we wanted flying cars. So a bunch of brilliant people drilled deep into the code of Cyberpunk 2077 and created a bunch of tools that let us do more.

The most prominent example of a core mod is the **Skyrim Script Extender** — fortunately, Cyberpunk 2077 is rather more stable.

Since this kind of mod interacts with the game on such a fundamental level, they are prone to **breaking** whenever CDPR changes their API. This is the reason why game updates break mods — we recommend to [**turn off auto-update**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates)
 and manually upgrading once the core mods you need have been brought up-to-date.

Existing core mods[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#popular-frameworks)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Redscript[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#redscript)

[Redscript](https://wiki.redmodding.org/redscript/)

Redscript ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/1511)
 | [GitHub](https://github.com/jac3km4/redscript)
) is an open-source programming language and toolkit (currently includes a compiler, a decompiler and a disassembler), natively working with Cyberpunk 2077's **scripting runtime**.

The scripts are compiled into a binary format recognizable by the game engine. The final blob includes original game scripts with all changes from the mods, and creates `r6/cache/final.redscripts.modded`

Redscript supports importing native types and functions registered by mods based on RED4ext.SDK, allowing modders to extend scripting runtime functionality.

Some examples for existing mod include, but are not limited to

*   changing combat balance
    
*   editing NPC behaviour
    
*   altering the interface
    
*   adding or removing UI widgets
    

### Cyber Engine Tweaks (CET)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#cyber-engine-tweaks)

[Cyber Engine Tweaks](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/)

**Requires RED4ext.**

Cyber Engine Tweaks ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/107) | [GitHub](https://github.com/yamashi/CyberEngineTweaks/releases)
) is a framework with a LUA wrapper, which will let you access anything exposed via RTTI, i.e. all scripted and native types and functions, including those added using redscript and RED4ext. On top of that, it lets you **bind hotkeys** for custom functions and interact with the game's TweakDB database at runtime.

Changes made via CET's Tweak Browser are not persistent, because the TweakDB gets changed at run-time. You can use TweakXL or create a CET mod to apply your changes each time the TweakDB is initialized.

Lots of mods and scripts run on top of CET, tweaking the game in various ways from changing the weather to adding a wardrobe system before CDPR added one.

The perhaps most prominent example is Appearance Menu Mod ([wiki](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained/amm)
 | [Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/790)
), which turns Cyberpunk 2077 into The Sims: Capitalist Dystopia.

### RED4ext[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#red4ext)

[RED4ext Plugin Dev](https://docs.red4ext.com/)

RED4ext ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/2380) | [GitHub](https://github.com/WopsS/RED4ext/releases)
) is Cyberpunk 2077's equivalent to Skyrim Script Extender. It consists of two parts:

**RED4ext Loader** – Loads and manages `.dll` plugins from `red4ext/plugins`. Provides essential functions such as game version checking, logging, hooking, and some REDengine 4 specific helpers for plugins.

**RED4ext.SDK** – Contains the reversed engineered types and helpers to extend the engine. It can also be used independently of RED4ext loader. Originally written in C++, but there's also a [Rust binding](https://github.com/jac3km4/red4ext-rs)
.

With RED4ext >= 1.13, you no longer need [cybercmd](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/5176)
. Make sure to **uninstall** it!

### ArchiveXL[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#archivexl)

[![Logo](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2Ffluidicon.png&width=20&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a9296982&sv=2)GitHub - psiberx/cp2077-archive-xl: Cyberpunk 2077 mod that allows you to expand game resources without conflicts.GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-archive-xl)

**Requires RED4ext.**

ArchiveXL ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/4198)
 | [GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-archive-xl/)
) allows modders to load custom resources into Cyberpunk 2077. Working under the hood, it is the essential tool to **add** and **extend** rather than **replace**. It also includes script extensions to, for example, load UI widgets from any resources than only those available to the current context.

For ArchiveXL specific documentation, check the [corresponding sub-page.](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained/archivexl)

### TweakXL[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#tweakxl--archivexl)

[![Logo](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2Ffluidicon.png&width=20&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a9296982&sv=2)GitHub - psiberx/cp2077-tweak-xl: Cyberpunk 2077 mod that allows you to modify TweakDB.GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-tweak-xl)

Requires RED4ext.

TweakXL ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/4197)
 | [GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-tweak-xl)
) allows modders to modify **TweakDB** in the form of `.yaml` or `.tweak` files. It also adds a scripting API allowing redscript mods to apply changes during TweakDB initialization or dynamically.

### Codeware[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#codeware)

Requires RED4ext.

Codeware ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/7780)
 | [GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-codeware)
) is a library and framework for redscript and Cyber Engine Tweaks mods. It exposes many engine types and functions that are not available by default and adds new functionalities regarding many aspects of the game, such as:

*   game events and life cycle
    
*   spawning game objects
    
*   manipulating world states
    
*   building UI widgets
    
*   accessing type information
    

... and much more.

### Equipment-EX[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#equipment-ex)

Equipment-EX ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/6945)
 | [GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-equipment-ex)
) makes use of every single one of the frameworks mentioned above to overhaul CDPR's wardrobe system. Extending it from 6 slots to more than 30, it lets you save an unlimited number of outfits and supports modded items.

### RedFileSystem[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#redfilesystem)

Requires RED4ext.

RedFileSystem ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/13378)
 | [GitHub](https://github.com/rayshader/cp2077-red-filesystem)
) is a library for redscript and Cyber Engine Tweaks mods. It exposes functions to read / write with the file system. It supports UTF8 text and Json formats. Modders can create, read and write files within game's directory. It can be useful to store custom data, for example in a mod's directory.

Log files for core mods[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#log-files-for-core-mods)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Logs: Cyber Engine Tweaks[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#logs-cyber-engine-tweaks)

This will tell you what mods and scripts CET is loading, and if there have been any errors in the process.

If all goes well, it will look like this:

### Logs: Redscript[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#logs-redscript)

It will tell you which `.reds` files it has been loading, and if any of them failed.

Please note the **warning** in the log:

*   If the mod is working, ignore it
    
*   If the mod isn't working, this tells you what to check. You can't have mods installed twice!
    

If all goes well, it will look like this:

### Logs: RED4ext[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#logs-red4ext)

It will tell you which of the plugin DLLs it has been loading.

If all goes well, it will look like this (version numbers are fake):

### Logs: ArchiveXL[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#logs-archivexl)

Tells you which .xl files have been found and processed and what it has been adding into the game files upon startup.

If it loads correctly, you will see something like this:

Note that the example contains a bunch of warnings. That indicates problems with the modded files (which I ignore, since they are working),

### Logs: TweakXL[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#logs-tweakxl)

Tells you which tweaks have been read and processed, pointing out errors and warnings in the process. If your custom item additions don't work, you might find a hint here.

If it loads correctly, you will see something like this:

### [](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained#undefined)

# Overview | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview.md)
.

Asset I/O[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#asset-i-o)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit includes an all-purpose import and export tool for converting assets to game files and vice versa. Our tooling is bespoke allowing for native conversions with extensive options to suit your needs (check [Import/Export](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/import-export)
 or find the list of [I/O formats for export/import](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/import-export/i-o-formats-for-export-import)
).

Base game and user-generated archives can be browsed on the fly, making game dumps obsolete. Our Asset Browser also features instant preview for assets such as meshes and textures.

Development[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#development)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is an open-source community developed application. The source code for WolvenKit and other projects can be found on our [GitHub page.](https://github.com/WolvenKit/)
 WolvenKit is primarily written in C# and the GUI uses WPF framework. Anyone can download the source code and build on their own with Visual Studio. If you're a developer who is interested in getting involved, please check out [Contributing](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/help/contributing)
!

File Editor[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#file-editor)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is built around our RTTI-based file editor. Any REDengine file can be inspected, edited, and saved with the File Editor. The File Editor is also capable of reading and writing in-place buffer data. Game files can also be converted to human-readable json and back for ease-of-use.

Projects[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#projects)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit can host virtually all of your required project files inside the Project Explorer. Each project is neatly separated into distinct folders which can be accessed through the application. No more whiplash from constantly navigating your file explorer to switch between projects! Feel free to host all of your assets within a mod project. Source files such as blends, psd, xcf, and more can be placed inside your project tree and opened directly with a double click! WolvenKit works with you by organizing your project files automatically.

UI/UX[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#ui-ux)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is created with the intention of being an all-purpose mod editor. The application interface is designed to help modders keep their sanity by having every tool at their disposal from the main editor. The application is built with a configurable interface to suit your preferences.

Workflows[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#workflows)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit features a robust build pipeline for handling REDengine assets. Game files can be browsed, extracted, modified, and packed into an archive once again for use in game. WolvenKit can help guide modders along with this process by using our efficient pipelines.

Other[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#other)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

### [Blender Integration](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/blender-integration)
[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/overview#undefined)

WolvenKit supports Blender for one-click imports of fully shaded Cyberpunk meshes using the Multilayered.mt supershader. Find the workflows here:

[Exporting Rigs & Anims](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/modding-community/exporting-to-blender/exporting-rigs-and-anims)

[Exporting Vehicles](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/modding-community/exporting-vehicles)

[PreviousHome](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit)
[NextRelease Notes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/features/release-notes)

Last updated 2 years ago

# Home | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home.md)
.

What's a Home good for?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#whats-a-home-good-for)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FNS7rAumrQfvUpsHjdl4b%252FWK%25208.7%2520Home%2520Generic.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3990ccbc-f64d-492c-ae07-cfc308dda45c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1fa0a36d&sv=2)

Welcome[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#welcome)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

When WolvenKit is launched the **Home** functions as bulletin board to help you pick up where you left off. Recent mod projects are displayed as a scrolling list of cards which can be directly clicked to get started. Other crucial functions for creating or opening projects are displayed in the right-hand column.

The WolvenKit icon can also be used to swap between Home and the Editor!

Mod Manager[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#mod-manager)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Manage REDmod installations and deployment

Plugins[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#plugins)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Download and update various [Home: Plugins](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home/home-plugins)
 and extensions for Cyberpunk 2077 modding

Settings[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#settings)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Settings can be accessed from the Home sidebar. Learn more about [**WolvenKit Settings here.**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#settings)

Web Content[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#web-content)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WolvenKit is capable of displaying web-based content natively. The Home page features several built-in web pages!

### Wiki[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#wiki)

This Wiki can be viewed from within WolvenKit. Hi WolvenKit users!

### GitHub[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#github)

GitHub can be used to directly track progress with WolvenKit development

### Website[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home#website)

Take a peek at our very own website, [RED Modding!](https://redmodding.org/)

[PreviousTroubleshooting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/troubleshooting)
[NextHome: Plugins](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home/home-plugins)

# Base Status Effects | Cyber Engine Tweaks

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects.md)
.

Working Effects[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#working-effects)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### AndroidTurnOff[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#androidturnoff)

*   Makes NPC T-Pose
    

### AndroidTurnOn[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#androidturnon)

*   Puts them in combat search state, but with a sleep status effect symbol over their head. Kinda weird
    

### BerserkNPCBuff[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#berserknpcbuff)

*   Works the same for both applying to NPC & Player
    
*   Unsure which is stronger this is BeserkPlayerBuff
    

### BerserkPlayerBuff[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#berserkplayerbuff)

*   Works the same for both applying to NPC & Player
    
*   Unsure which is stronger this is BeserkNPCBuff
    

### Burning[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#burning)

### Bleeding[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#bleeding)

### Blind[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#blind)

### CPO\_Shocked[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#cpo_shocked)

### CombatStim[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#combatstim)

*   Visual fx from healing
    

### Cloaked[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#cloaked)

*   Only sound effect plays for both Player & NPC
    

### CloakedOda[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#cloakedoda)

*   Only sound effect plays for both Player & NPC
    

### CyberwareMalfunction[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#cyberwaremalfunction)

*   CyberwareMalfunctionLvl2
    
*   CyberwareMalfunctionLvl3
    

### Defeat[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#defeat)

### Drugged[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#drugged)

*   Freezes an NPC in place, Player gets a tiny visual effect
    

### Drunk[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#drunk)

*   Gives visual effect only to Player
    

### Electrocuted[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#electrocuted)

### EMP[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#emp)

### Encumbered[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#encumbered)

*   Effects NPCs weirdly, had me blow up in flames after hitting me...
    

### ForceKill[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#forcekill)

### Grappled[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#grappled)

*   Briefly puts weapon away then pulls it back out. Same for both NPCs and Player.
    

### GlowingTattoos[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#glowingtattoos)

### Invulnerable[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#invulnerable)

### KenjutsuBleeding[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#kenjutsubleeding)

### KerenzikovPlayerBuff[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#kerenzikovplayerbuff)

### LocomotionMalfunctionLevel4Passive[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#locomotionmalfunctionlevel4passive)

*   Works similar as encumbered, but puts target in combat state
    

### MemoryWipeLevel2[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#memorywipelevel2)

*   Works as expected on NPCs
    
*   Slight visual/sound fx and combat state enabled for Player
    

### NetwatcherGeneral[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#netwatchergeneral)

*   Glitch/Hacked visual effect
    

### OutOfOxygen[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#outofoxygen)

*   Removes player drowning/out of oxygen
    

### Overload[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#overload)

*   Works as expected when applied to NPC
    
*   Visual fx for Player no damage is done
    

### VehicleHighDamageOverTime[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#vehiclehighdamageovertime)

### VehicleLowDamageOverTime[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#vehiclelowdamageovertime)

### SecondaryKnockdown[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#secondaryknockdown)

*   Same effect as getting hit by a car but no damage
    
*   Sets NPCs into combat mode only, no actual visual knockdown occurs
    

### SecondHeart[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#secondheart)

*   Visual effect and some stat boost that I couldn't figure out maybe a health boost. After that a stun effect ocurs briefly.
    

### SetFriendly[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#setfriendly)

*   Puts NPC into cyberpsychosis state
    
*   Visual/sound fx for Player
    

### Stun[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#stun)

### SystemCollapse[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#systemcollapse)

*   Instakills NPC, looks like both overload effect
    
*   Sound fx when applied to Player but no damage is done
    

### SuicideWithWeapon[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/base-status-effects#suicidewithweapon)

*   Puts Player in combat mode.
    
*   Makes NPC commit suicide

# Error Codes | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#summary)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Published**: May 1 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)
 **Last documented update**: Jan 6 2025 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)

This page contains an overview about Wolvenkit's internal error codes and what they mean.

For **developers**:

When **editing** this page (especially section headers), please update the internal mapping: `WolvenKit/Helpers/LogCodeHelper.cs`

What to do with an error[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#what-to-do-with-an-error)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you ended up on this page, you ran into a problem that needs to be fixed inside WolvenKit. You now have the following options:

### Install the Nightly[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#install-the-nightly)

If you are on the stable release, you should try [The Wolvenkit Nightly](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/download/the-wolvenkit-nightly)
. There's a good chance that your problem is already solved.

### Update all tools involved[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#update-all-tools-involved)

Make sure that you are on the most recent version of whatever tool's you're using. This includes, but is not limited to, the [Wolvenkit Blender IO Suite](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit-blender-io-suite)
 and [MLSETUP Builder](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/mlsetup-builder)
.

### Find support on Discord[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#find-support-on-discord)

Find our [discord server](http://discord.gg/redmodding)
 and hit up the `**#wolvenkit-support**` channel. (If you aren't on the Nightly yet, you should really do that now, because it's the first thing everyone will tell you).

### Create a ticket[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#create-a-ticket)

If your error still happens in the Nightly, you need to **tell us about it**. It's easy: bugs that don't get reported don't get fixed.

Head to Wolvenkit's github page and [create a ticket](https://github.com/WolvenKit/Wolvenkit/issues)
 (you need a github or Google account, but they won't send you spam).

To fix the bug, we need to **watch it in action**. Please include everything we need to make it happen — at the very least, we need the **most recent log file**:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FldIiQWGKzCh30GclPivv%252Fwkit_open_log_folder.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9df93a77-533e-4c99-b211-3ce5c12faeaa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=da2537ea&sv=2)

Error code list[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#error-code-list)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If the steps below don't help, please get in touch via [Discord](https://discord.com/invite/redmodding)
, [create a ticket](https://github.com/WolvenKit/Wolvenkit/issues)
, or browse the [Troubleshooting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/troubleshooting)
 page. Keep your most recent log file ready!

### 0x2000: Type not supported (8192)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x2000-type-not-supported-8192)

WolvenKit ran into a problem during internal conversion. Here's what you can do:

1.  Install [The Wolvenkit Nightly](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/download/the-wolvenkit-nightly)
    
2.  Re-create whatever file you were trying to work on at that time:
    
    1.  A game file from an earlier patch: Add it to your project again
        
    2.  A depot file: Delete the file from the depot and/or [re-create the depot](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/create-depot#steps-partial-depot)
        
    3.  A raw file: re-create the file with the latest version of whatever tool you used (Wolvenkit, the [Wolvenkit Blender IO Suite](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit-blender-io-suite)
        ...)
        
    

### 0x3002: Resources plugin[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x3002-resources-plugin)

The [Wolvenkit Resources plugin](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home/home-plugins#wolvenkit-resources)
 may have become corrupted. Open Home -> Plugins and remove it, then restart Wolvenkit, reinstall the plugin, and try again.

### 0x5000: Invalid settings[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x5000-invalid-settings)

You have an unspecified issue with your settings. Double-check them and make sure that everything is configured correctly.

If that doesn't help, remove or re-name your settings file and restart WolvenKit:

### 0x5001: Invalid Game File Executable[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x5001-invalid-game-file-executable)

Check your [Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings)
 ->[Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#game-executable-.exe-path)
 and make sure that it points at your `Cyberpunk2077.exe`. For more information, check the wiki link.

### 0x5002: Failed to launch game executable[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x5002-failed-to-launch-game-executable)

Check [0x5001: Invalid Game File Executable](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x5001-invalid-game-file-executable)
 and make sure that all your settings are valid. If that doesn't fix your problem, you may have to reset your settings, see [0x5000: Invalid settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#id-0x5000-invalid-settings)
 for detes.

MTO requirement[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/error-codes#mto-requirement)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You are here because a popup in Wolvenkit told you about **Material and Texture Override**.

The mod is necessary because Cyberpunk caches all of the base game's `.mlsetup` files, so your changes won't be visible without it. You can [get it on Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/25246)
.

The original MTO can crash the game under certain circumstances, so you should remove it and install the new version instead.

To **avoid the dependency**, you can use [Resource Patching: Mesh Appearances](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained/archivexl/archivexl-resource-patching/archivexl-patching-appearances)
 .

[NextWait, it can do that?](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/wait-it-can-do-that)

Last updated 9 months ago

# Analysing other mods | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods.md)
.

This guide will walk you through **analysing or extracting .archive files** from tutorial resources or other mods.

### Required: [WolvenKit](https://github.com/WolvenKit/WolvenKit/releases)
[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#required-wolvenkit)

Please keep in mind that you are looking at somebody else's work. **Always credit** if you use anything from other peoples' mods, unless it's explicitly stated that you don't have to!

Some modders don't want their mods to be unbundled. Most people don't mind if you do it for your own personal use, but you should seek permission and respect their decision.

For a documentation on how to use Wolvenkit CLI, see [here](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods/legacy-analysing-other-mods-with-wolvenkit-console)
. Unless you have a very good reason to use the console, you probably don't want to.

If you want to **edit** files, you need a [Wolvenkit Project](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/wolvenkit-projects)
. If you don't know how to make one, you can check [R&R: Your own Wolvenkit project](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/recolours-and-refits/r-and-r-your-own-wolvenkit-project)

### Step 1: Switch to mod browser[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#step-1-switch-to-mod-browser)

Once you have [installed the mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077)
 or added it to your [extra mod directory](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#additional-mod-directory)
, you can use Wolvenkit's [Mod Browser](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/asset-browser#mod-browser)
to browse it:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-bf6a8077105e2ad812f64b895cc4e90022151a67%252Fview_mods_with_wkit.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e5244ea0&sv=2)

Switch to "Mod browser"

You can now add the content to your project like regular game files.

If you can't (because everything is scrambled), check [Troubleshooting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#troubleshooting)
.

### Step 2: Find the correct files[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#step-2-find-the-correct-files)

You can check [Wolvenkit Search: Finding files](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/wolvenkit-search-finding-files)
 for how to find and filter everything.

The folders in the mod browser correspond to the names of the .archive files you have installed.

E.g., To find all `.mlsetup` (colour definition) files inside the [Netrunning Suits mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/recolours-and-refits)
, search for:

If you want all mesh files from the [Custom T-Shirt Mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/adding-new-items)
, search for

f you want to know more, you can read up on [Wolvenkit's search](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/wolvenkit-search-finding-files)
.

### Step 3: Add the files to your project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#step-3-add-the-files-to-your-project)

Select the file that you want to add by checking the box. Then right-click on it, and click on the `Add selected items to project` option.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-a4c93bc31918d92171d1f56a0a8b1e95b68fd806%252Fbrowsing_mods_add_to_project.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2378eaf5&sv=2)

Add items to project

That's it! You now have the file in your mod project and can start editing.

Troubleshooting[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#troubleshooting)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### I can't add anything to my project![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#i-cant-add-anything-to-my-project)

Make sure that you actually have a Wolvenkit project opened (you will see the project name in the top-right corner next to the close button). Can't add files to a project if there's no project!

### All file names are numbers / there are only bin files[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#all-file-names-are-numbers-there-are-only-bin-files)

You have found a mod that is not compatible with the current hashing algorithm. Long (and sad) story short, from time to time that needs an update, and then anything packed with a different version of the algorithm won't resolve anymore.

The good news is that **you can still use those files**. The bad news is that you have to guess which is which.

To try and resolve that, switch to the mod browser, and click the crosshair button next to the search bar. This will scan your mods for a way to resolve those hashes.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-abefade16743d042d24f35aade04d2c869779b2e%252Fanalysing_mods_broken_hashes.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d7e5810a&sv=2)

If that doesn't work, you can try opening the `.archive` with Wolvenkit [8.9](https://github.com/WolvenKit/WolvenKit/releases/tag/8.9.0)
 or [8.7](https://github.com/WolvenKit/WolvenKit/releases/tag/8.7.0)
 - anything older will be incompatible with game versions >= 1.6.

If neither of these work, then you'll have to try and guess.

#### Didn't work[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods#didnt-work)

Some authors don't want other people to look at their files and deliberately corrupt their archives to prevent Wolvenkit from reading them. The game is a lot more robust than WKit, so these mods usually work, although side effects can't be ruled out. If none of this works, you can still look at the files and their properties in the [File Editor](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/file-editor)
 and try to guess what's what. Other than that, you're out of luck, though.

[PreviousGetting started: Making mods](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/getting-started-making-mods)
[NextLegacy: Analysing other mods with Wolvenkit Console](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/analysing-other-mods/legacy-analysing-other-mods-with-wolvenkit-console)

. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound.md)
.

This section contains guide about changing sounds in the game.

[Finding an audio file](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound/finding-an-audio-file)
[Modding Events Metadata](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound/audio-modding-basics)
[Replacing sounds effects](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound/replacing-sounds-effects)
[Playing Sounds in-game: Basics](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound/playing-sounds-in-game-basics)

If you wish to read about how the game handles audio see [the theoretical page](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/audio-files)
.

External links:[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound#external-links)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

There are a couple of pages about sounds on [Rom's](mailto:undefined)
 wiki for his Addicted Mod:

[https://cyb3rpsych0s1s.github.io/4ddicted/cheatsheet/sounds.html](https://cyb3rpsych0s1s.github.io/4ddicted/cheatsheet/sounds.html)
 [https://cyb3rpsych0s1s.github.io/4ddicted/travelog/2023-03-31.html](https://cyb3rpsych0s1s.github.io/4ddicted/travelog/2023-03-31.html)

Bioskop's comprehensive guide to vocoding - [Google Docs](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1h_far0MPnKtxnhuxUWLiQduqNw6dxgr2qSNB84dCkEQ/edit#heading=h.amjq0j2yglxw)

Last, but not least, you can now also use [Audioware](https://github.com/cyb3rpsych0s1s/audioware/releases)
 native plugin to add, customize and play custom sounds at your convenience with scripts. Audioware also provides [its guide](https://cyb3rpsych0s1s.github.io/audioware/)
 to get you up-and-running in no-time!

[PreviousArchived: Removing Occlusion Nodes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/archived-guides/removing-occlusion)
[NextFinding an audio file](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/sound/finding-an-audio-file)

Last updated 1 year ago

# Settings | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#summary)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Last documented update: Jun 17 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)
 (Wolvenkit 8.14 Nightly)

This page documents Wolvenkit's settings menu, and what the individual entries mean.

TL;DR[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#tl-dr)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

You **must** make sure that your Game Executable Path is set. Wolvenkit will not work otherwise.

Setting

value

Game Executable Path (.exe)

Path to the game's executable file on your hard drive, e.g.

`C:\Games\Cyberpunk 2077\bin\x64\Cyberpunk2077.exe`

Depot Path

Defaults to `C:\Users\yourusername\AppData\Roaming\REDModding\WolvenKit\Depot`

A folder where Wolvenkit can store all its clutter. **NOT** your game directory.

Settings: Overview[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#settings-overview)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For a textual explanation of the seettings, see [the next section](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#settings-explained)

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252F1yrZmaZuzSxYLI9nn7YS%252F8.9.1%2520settings%2520generic.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc75d1f73-9649-4564-9d44-9b8ea6b52108&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=18dd0fad&sv=2)

Example settings window for 8.9.1

Cyberpunk[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#cyberpunk)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Game Executable Path[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#game-executable-path)

You **must** set this.

The path to your `Cyberpunk2077.exe` , inside [The Cyberpunk 2077 Game Directory](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
 under `bin\x64`.

If Cyberpunk is installed within Program Files, we recommend running WolvenKit as administrator.

### Launch Command[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#launch-command)

Optional

The path to your `Cyberpunk2077.exe` , inside [The Cyberpunk 2077 Game Directory](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
. Will be auto-generated from [Game Executable Path](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#game-executable-path)
.

You can also put the path to the REDLauncher here, if you want that (but why would you?)

### Launch Options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#launch-options)

Optional

Arguments for the executable. You can find a list in [the yellow wiki](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/)
 on the [Launch parameters](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory/launch-parameters)
 page.

### Depot Path[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#depot-path)

You **should** set this, or Wolvenkit will default to your AppSettings directory. Since this is on the same drive as your Windows install, it can clutter up your harddrive.

The **Depot Path** is a WolvenKit system folder for caching game assets. It serves as a cache for mesh exports with materials. WolvenKit builds a repository of visual assets within the Depot for usage with external applications such as MLSetupBuilder or Blender.

By default, it is set to `C:\Users\yourusername\AppData\Roaming\REDModding\WolvenKit\Depot`, but you can pick any location. It does not need to be on an SSD, although Wolvenkit might load faster if it is.

Learn more about the [**Material exports here**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/blender-integration)
**.**

### Additional Mod Directory[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#additional-mod-directory)

Optional

A directory for .archive mods outside of your game directory, for example for resources that you want to load for multiple projects. Mods inside this folder will show up in the [Mod Browser](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/asset-browser#mod-browser)
 **after** your installed mods.

Display[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#display)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Show CName hashes as hex[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-cname-hashes-as-hex)

Display hashes for CNames as hexadecimal (rather than decimal)

### Show ResourcePath hashes as hex[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-resourcepath-hashes-as-hex)

Display hashes for ResourcePaths as hexadecimal (rather than decimal)

### Show NodeRef hashes as hex[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-noderef-hashes-as-hex)

Display hashes for NodeRefs as hexadecimal (rather than decimal)

### Show TweakDBID hashes as hex[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-tweakdbid-hashes-as-hex)

Display hashes for TweakDBID entries as hexadecimal (rather than decimal)

### Show reference graph[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-reference-graph)

### Game language used for LocKeys[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#game-language-used-for-lockeys)

Lets you customize the language for the [LocKey Browser](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/lockey-browser)
. Default: `en_us`

### Show Graph Editor Node Properties[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-graph-editor-node-properties)

Graph Editor: Detailed display?

File Editor[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#file-editor)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Group Large Collections[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#group-large-collections)

Toggles limited collections for improved performance within the File Editor when working when navigating large files.

### Group Size[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#group-size)

Adjusts the amount of elements of in a limited collection within the File Editor.

### Default to Simple Mode[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#default-to-simple-mode)

Starting with 1.14, Wolvenkit offers multiple editor modes. You can find details under [Switching Editor Modes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/file-editor#switching-editor-modes)
.

### Ignored Extensions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#ignored-extensions)

File extensions that you want to open in an external editor

General[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#general)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### **Do not check for updates**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#do-not-check-for-updates)

Will disable Wolvenkit's auto-update

### **Show Guided Tour**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-guided-tour)

UNKNOWN FUNCTIONALITY This toggle may not work in the latest WolvenKit build.

### **Update Channel**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#update-channel)

The update channel determines which type WolvenKit updates are received.

*   **Stable** WolvenKit will only show prompts when new releases are published (recommended)
    
*   **Nightly** WolvenKit will show the latest development builds which may be unstable
    

### Your name[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#your-name)

Will be used for automatically generated subfolders / item names. If you configure this, it'll be auto-filled for new projects.

### Your e-Mail[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#your-e-mail)

Will be used when creating new projects.

### **Theme Accent**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#theme-accent)

Changes the accent color of UI elements throughout WolvenKit.

Interface[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#interface)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Lets you customize Wolvenkit's interface.

### Import/Export: Show advanced options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#import-export-show-advanced-options)

Turn this off unless you know what you're doing

### Update references on rename[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#update-references-on-rename)

The default state of this box when you open the rename dialogue (shortcut: `F2`)

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FHk8gKjAHT9yYGaEaIYEL%252Fupdate_in_project_files_references.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dde8532ad-d7d1-4bb1-b231-6505b10dffa2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6e443d5d&sv=2)

### Show REDMod in Ribbon[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-redmod-in-ribbon)

Adds the REDMod buttons back to the ribbon. (Why do you want this?)

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FfXRv3RRAra9008WSOLqb%252Fshow_redmod_in_ribbon.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbf23cbb2-6341-46ed-a975-0fa580f6181e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7dd3f36a&sv=2)

### Show File Preview[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-file-preview)

You can disable this feature to save performance, or simply hide the panel.

Enables interactive Quick Previews within the Properties panel when navigating the Asset Browser and Project Explorer.

### Append default launch profiles[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#append-default-launch-profiles)

Will append the [Default profiles](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu/launch-profiles#default-profiles)
 to the list of launch profiles if they are not defined (this will not overwrite your custom profiles)

[PreviousFile Validation](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation)
[NextUsage](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage)

# File Validation | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#summary)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Last documented update**: March 12 2025 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)

This page will tell you how to use and configure the File Validation feature (added in 8.9.1)

This feature has an **impact on performance** — every time you save, there will be a short lag while the file validation does its thing. You can turn this off (check [The automatic hook](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#the-automatic-hook)
).

What is it?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#what-is-it)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This feature will try to validate the internal structure and — based on your settings — dependent files.

By default, it will trigger every time you save a file. You can turn this off (check [The automatic hook](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#the-automatic-hook)
) and trigger it by hand (see below).

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252F6CpdWwl0RLDsWSdJWW7l%252Ffile_validation_in_mesh.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbd00c18c-5f43-40f0-8e1a-da472c2fbe7c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2c1bfcfd&sv=2)

The log will yell at you if your files have issues now

Running File Validation[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#running-file-validation)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### In the editor[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#in-the-editor)

Inside the [editor](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor)
, you can run File Validation from the menu bar:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FVlcH2KC87uo0ybn6WmCc%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dff763ed6-ca7a-475e-9ee1-3627d3fd03d0&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=710bd350&sv=2)

### On the entire project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#on-the-entire-project)

From the Project Menu in the main bar, you can run File Validation on the entire project.

Since this can take a long time, Wolvenkit will show you a warning first.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FJKYSe9HGCq6Qs7W1K3Yb%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De22869b0-80c4-4226-8c0c-da4371c8fb9d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9b58876e&sv=2)

The automatic hook[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#the-automatic-hook)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

By default, File Validation will trigger every time you save. Since this can impact performance for larger files, you might want to disable it.

### I don't want it, how do I get rid of it?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#i-dont-want-it-how-do-i-get-rid-of-it)

In the [Script Manager](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/tools/script-manager)
, switch to the `Hooks` tab and uncheck the box behind `hook_global`:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FX8RdqGOVw1bYnkCy6ovJ%252Fdisabling_file_validation.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D40043b9c-5aaa-4358-9a85-8063f04b8e10&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=55d515dd&sv=2)

For details, see [Configuring File Validation](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#configuring-file-validation)
 below.

There was an error[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#there-was-an-error)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you're here because of a link in the Wolvenkit console, there's nothing to worry about, your file saved fine! However, you've managed to run into something that the program isn't equipped to handle yet.

If you would like to help us fix it, you can open a ticket on [github](https://github.com/WolvenKit/Wolvenkit/issues)
 or create a forum post on our [Discord server](https://discord.gg/redmodding)
 in #wkit-bugs-and-requests.

Please attach a minimal version of your project's source folder (we only need `archive`, but all involved files have to be present, or we won't be able to reproduce your exact error).

Configuring File Validation[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#configuring-file-validation)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

There is no fancy UI for the configuration right now (sorry for that), but you can fine-tune file validation via [Script Manager](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/tools/script-manager)
.

You need to change the settings in`hook_settings.wscript`. This is how you open it:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FyWBv7p2fIw7w0KIVL3AA%252Ffile_validation_open_settings.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D14d00b67-2104-40eb-be6b-5c65b4808f37&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=580e1191&sv=2)

That will open the script editor panel, which looks like this:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FBdBoe4g6J8r5CfPzfZXJ%252Ffile_validation_hook_settings.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D7ff97f18-535d-48d6-bc8b-eea7d0f575fd&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a665cc0e&sv=2)

You can toggle features on and off by switching values between `true` and `false`. Be careful that you don't delete any commas!

In an ideal world, this wiki is always up-to-date with the existing source code. However, that doesn't happen automatically (and can't easily be made that way), and the Wolvenkit team focuses on fixing bugs and developing new features.

The most up-to-date documentation will always be **in the source code**. Read the comment blocks (green in the screenshot above) for a description of what a flag does.

Here, you will see a list of settings, which are documented in the next section.

### Global and shared settings[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#global-and-shared-settings)

#### Depot paths[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#depot-paths)

File Validation will check if any given depot path exists, and warn you if the file could not be found.

This **will** give false positives if you're on the mod browser!

#### Enabled[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#enabled)

The flag `Enabled` in line 4 will enable/disable file validation globally.

Each entry in the list has an individual `Enabled` flag that lets you turn off validation for this particular type of file.

#### DisableAutofix[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#disableautofix)

File validation can automatically correct certain errors for you. There are certain advanced use cases where you'd want to turn this off — if you don't know about them, then you'll probably want this.

Right now, this is set to `false` by default and affects the following toggles:

*   [Workspot.fixIndexOrder](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#fixindexorder)
    
*   [Workspot.autoReopenFile](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#autoreopenfile)
    

#### validateRecursively[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#validaterecursively)

This is the reason behind the micro freeze each time you save a file. If it is enabled, then Wolvenkit will also look at included files — for example, if you save an .app file, it will collect all included meshes (via components or via root entity) and warn you about missing appearances.

You can disable this to save performance, but that will reduce file validation to a glorified typo checker for your file paths. Its developer strongly advises against it, as the feature has saved her **hours** of frustration already, and at the point of writing it hasn't even been released.

### Anims: animation files (.anim)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#anims-animation-files-.anim)

Checks if all animation names are unique. Since animations are targeted by name, this is the #1 reason why your mod will show the wrong animation.

#### **checkForDuplicates**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkforduplicates)

Will warn you about duplicate animation names (e.g. animations 2 and 31 are both named "facepalm")

### App: app files (.app)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#app-app-files-.app)

Checks all appearances and their associated `partsOverrides` and `partsValues`.

#### **checkComponentNameDuplication**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkcomponentnameduplication)

Will warn you about duplicate component names. Components are targeted by name, so they should be unique for each appearance or mesh entity.

#### checkForCrashyDependencies[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkforcrashydependencies)

inplaceResources can crash your game (it's complicated and has something to do with timing and async loading). This flag will toggle the check.

#### checkPotentialOverrideCollisions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkpotentialoverridecollisions)

Will warn you if components with the same name are pointing to different meshes.

#### checkCookPaths[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkcookpaths)

Will print a warning if you have cookedOverrides set in the root or in individual appearances. These things are the reason why mods such as cookedAppsNulled exist.

### csv (ArchiveXL factory files)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#csv-archivexl-factory-files)

File validation assumes that this is a **factory** for ArchiveXL or similar. If it isn't, you can ignore any warnings, or [create an issue](https://github.com/WolvenKit/WolvenKit/issues)
 so that we can add your use case.

#### checkProjectResourcePaths[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkprojectresourcepaths)

Assumes that entries are file paths. Will print a warning if the files can't be found.

#### warnAboutInvalidDepotPaths[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#warnaboutinvaliddepotpaths)

Prints warnings for everything that doesn't look like it's a file path (by regular expression checking, there should be at least one slash)

### ent: Root or mesh entity[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#ent-root-or-mesh-entity)

Similar to the .app file, this will validate nested files, basically checking the entire ArchiveXL chain of dependencies.

#### validateRecursively[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#validaterecursively-1)

This is really performant and will only look into each file once. That being said, the more files you pull in, the longer it takes, and you might want to disable this feature. On the other hand, saving the root entity is a great way to find out what exactly is broken about your ArchiveXL project.

#### checkComponentNameDuplication[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkcomponentnameduplication-1)

Same as in the app file: will warn you about duplicate component names. Components are targeted by name, so they should be unique for each appearance or mesh entity.

#### checkDynamicAppearanceTag[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkdynamicappearancetag)

Will print a warning that you might have forgotten to add a DynamicAppearances tag to your root entity if either of the following conditions is true:

*   There are empty appearance names in your root entity
    
*   Your .app partsOverrides appearance names or your mesh entity use dynamic string substitution
    

… since ArchiveXL won't work its magic if you haven't set the tag.

### Json: Localization files[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#json-localization-files)

Will try to warn you about issues that will cause empty or incorrect strings to be displayed

#### checkDuplicateKeys[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkduplicatekeys)

This will warn you if any entries will overwrite each other due to shared key data.

#### checkEmptyFemaleVariant[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkemptyfemalevariant)

By default, the game uses `femaleVariant` - `maleVariant` will only be considered if you use either the [gender suffix](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/file-formats/entity-.ent-files#what-are-suffixes)
 or [dynamic ArchiveXL appearances](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained/archivexl#dynamic-variants)
.

### Mesh: 3d object[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#mesh-3d-object)

Will check your mesh's internal material assignment. Will warn you about missing files, unresolved dependencies or errors with definitions and file types (some of which might potentially crash your game).

#### validateMaterialsRecursively[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#validatematerialsrecursively)

Will also check .mi files and their material setup. As the .mi chains can be quite long, this can cause performance issues.

#### checkDuplicateMaterialDefinitions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkduplicatematerialdefinitions)

Will warn you if two of your materials appear to be identical

### Mi: Material Template[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#mi-material-template)

Will check the material setup.

#### validateMaterialsRecursively[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#validatematerialsrecursively-1)

Will check nested .mi files and their material setup, if you are using any.

### Workspot: loading .anim files[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#workspot-loading-.anim-files)

Mostly for use with [this tutorial](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/animations/animations/amm-collab-anims-poses)
. Will check if the workspot pulls in existing .anim files, warning you about duplicate definitions.

#### fixIndexOrder[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#fixindexorder)

Convenience: Will automatically set indices of defined animations for you. There are advanced use cases where you might not want this. If you don't know what they are, you might want to turn on [autoReopenFile](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#autoreopenfile)
 in addition to this.

#### autoReopenFile[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#autoreopenfile)

More convenience: Automatically close and re-open the file after using `fixIndexOrder` (instead of bothering you about it). Default is `false`.

#### showUnusedAnimsInFiles[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#showunusedanimsinfiles)

Will warn you about animations in the included .anim files that aren't used by the workspot, in case you want to delete them.

#### showUndefinedWorkspotAnims[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#showundefinedworkspotanims)

Shows animations that you include in your workspot, but which aren't defined in any of the included .anims files.

#### checkIdleAnimNames[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkidleanimnames)

Will warn you if the idle anim name deviates from the appearance anim name. This isn't strictly necessary, but since the idle anim name is displayed in the list, you will never find the right appearance otherwise.

#### checkIdDuplication[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkidduplication)

Only triggers if you aren't using `fixIndexOrder`: this will warn you about duplicate numeric ID assignments. These will cause the game to mix up your animations.

#### checkFilepaths[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation#checkfilepaths)

Checks the files included via `finalAnimSets`, warning you about any loading errors (invalid file paths, invalid extensions, you forgot to add the entry to loadingHandles)
