# Downgrading / Preventing Auto-Updates | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates.md)
.

Things fall down when you let go of them, and mods break whenever a game updates. That is due to [framework compatibility](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained)
.

**Always make sure that your framework mods are up-to-date.**

You should generally wait with updating to the latest game version until all mods are up-to-date. If it's too late for that, you can try [downgrading](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#downgrading)
 Cyberpunk, or wait until the frameworks update.

Preventing the game from auto-updating[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#preventing-the-game-from-auto-updating)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### GOG Galaxy[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#gog-galaxy)

In GOG Galaxy, this can be done as a global setting — you disable auto-update for all of your games — or as a per-game option for some games like Cyberpunk 2077.

#### Only disable updates for CP2077[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#only-disable-updates-for-cp2077)

Open the GOG Galaxy page for Cyberpunk 2077 and click the ![manage icon](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-bec3f3e07fc85b857fec03636f6757aa0bd58a0b%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=35&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=308e1f71&sv=2)manage icon next to the big play button, then `Manage installation` and `Configure...` Disable the `Automatically update to the newest version` checkbox. Optionally, select the game patch you want to play on here. \\

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-2bd8c6967da3e502633560381c516e3eaa99f760%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3753da15&sv=2)

#### Globally disable updates[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#globally-disable-updates)

Open the settings and select the entry `Game features` in the sidebar, then uncheck the box for `Auto-update games`.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-6580c22444c39b11e1e9e1fb09ee488edd2f0eba%252Fgog-auto-update.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8aa14b13&sv=2)

### Epic[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#epic)

In the EPIC launcher, this is a global setting — you can only disable auto-update for all of your games.

Open the settings menu and scroll down to `MANAGE GAMES`, then disable auto-updates.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-9dd323f38f2cc93337f47e74ad8678b8e2d39165%252Fepic-auto-update.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a8068657&sv=2)

### Steam[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#steam)

Unlike the competition, Steam will not give you any control about auto-updating. For that reason, you need to forbid it from writing to a specific file on disk, without which it will be literally unable to update. Here's how:

1.  Find the `steamapps` folder (:
    
    1.  Right-click on Cyberpunk 2077 in your Steam Library and select `**Properties**`
        
    2.  On the side bar, click on `**Local Files**`
        
    3.  Select `**Browse**`. This will take you to Cyberpunk's install directory, which is **inside** the folder we want (e.g. `C:\Steam\steamapps\common\Cyberpunk 2077`)
        
    4.  Navigate two levels up (in windows explorer this is done by clicking the up arrow at the top left of the window, next to the file path, or by using the shortcut Alt + Up Arrow Key)
        
    
2.  Find the file `appmanifest_1091500.acf`
    
3.  Set the file to read-only:
    
    1.  Right-click on the file and select Properties
        
    2.  At the bottom of the dialogue, check the Read-only box
        
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-acfabfae3e3904c20c9386ef8b9b5438f8c4b6e9%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=81dc3f20&sv=2)

As long as Steam can't write to this file, it can't update Cyberpunk. Once you are ready to update, simply uncheck the Read-only checkbox again.

Don't forget to deactivate it again later!

Downgrading[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#downgrading)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### GOG[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#gog)

1.  [Disable auto-updates](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#gog)
    
2.  Head to the Cyberpunk game detail page
    
3.  Find the settings button next to the Play button
    
4.  Select `Manage Installation` -> `Configure…`
    
5.  Select the game version
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-362c83128a20e2165301d159cca903c99801d425%252Fgog-select-game-version.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a6be0490&sv=2)

### Epic[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#epic-1)

Sorry, choomba, you're fucked. No downgrading possible.

### Steam[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#steam-1)

Since 2.2, Steam supports downgrading to version `1.63` , `2.13` , `2.21` or `2.3`

Right-click on the game in Steam and select Properties, then go here:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-43810b8ec45c4435faaea16ba77aef1293c45634%252F2025.09.11-19.57.05_steamwebhelper.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=80c5af0c&sv=2)

For downgrading to other versions, see the expandable box below.

Using steamdb to attempt to download a previous depot[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#using-steamdb-to-attempt-to-download-a-previous-depot)

Longer instructions can be found [in this MakeUseOf guide](https://www.makeuseof.com/how-to-downgrade-steam-games/)
, but the condensed version (courtesy of our lovely Discord community) goes as follows: First [turn autoupdate off for Steam](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/users-downgrading-preventing-auto-updates#preventing-the-game-from-auto-updating)
 _**now**_ so that you don't forget to do it later. We'll wait. Here are the version-specific commands that you need to run. If you can't find your version on the list, check [here](https://steamdb.info/depot/1091501/history/)
 (Base Game) and [here](https://steamdb.info/depot/2138330/history/)
 (Phantom Liberty), and find the new Manifest ID tag.

**Patch 2.3:**

*   Base Game: `download_depot 1091500 1091501 6814019010317238099`
    
*   Phantom Liberty: `download_depot 2138330 2138330 2681614462109514084`
    
*   REDmod: `download_depot 1091500 2060310 6234713401507336211`
    

**Patch 2.21:**

*   Base Game: `download_depot 1091500 1091501 8420445566849588826`
    
*   Phantom Liberty: `download_depot 2138330 2138330 6387618923890239308`
    
*   REDmod: `download_depot 1091500 2060310 5840271637798999751`
    

Now, we do the thing:

1.  Open Steam
    
2.  Press `Win+R`
    
3.  Type `steam://open/console` to get into the steamdb console
    
4.  In the console, enter this command exactly to start the download (it'll be the entire 60 GB so be prepared): `download_depot 1091500 1091501 3807385820978085162`
    
5.  After the game finishes downloading, go to the installation location listed in the console, and copy either just the changed files (you can view the manifest for the new version) or the whole thing over your game directory to overwrite the new changes.
    
6.  If you have the Phantom Liberty DLC, you will need to do the steps 4 and 5 again for the DLC itself with the command: `download_depot 2138330 2138330 5094071097429923087`
    

This should leave you with the correct previous game version. You can right-click cyberpunk2077.exe to verify the versions in Properties > Details.

You might still need to do a script cache reset or other cleanup before the game starts up nice, so head on over to [those instructions](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/user-guide-troubleshooting#troubleshooting-after-an-update)
 if need be.

[PreviousChange logs](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/change-logs)

# Menu | WolvenKit

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu.md)
.

What is the Menu?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#what-is-the-menu)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Menu is the main navigation bar at the top of the WolvenKit application. The Menu contains key functionality such as saving, opening files, and building mods. Read below for details about each Menu button. The Menu also displays the current mod project name and file opened with WolvenKit.

### Toolbar[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#toolbar)

The [Toolbar](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#toolbar)
 is available directly below the Menu from the Editor. The buttons add convenience shortcuts to certain menu functions.

Additionally, the Toolbar contains the **Launch Profiles** button.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FWNqhU952VsFDiaF96rFH%252FWK%25208.7%2520Menu%2520and%2520Toolbar%2520Generic.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9431ff45-af6d-4298-b3cd-5f051767d1c4&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fcafc760&sv=2)

HOME[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#home)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

Shows the WolvenKit [**Home**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/home)
 view

File[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#file)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

### New File[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#new-file)

Opens the dialogue to create a new file.

### Save[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#save)

Save the currently selected document from the File Viewer

### Save As[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#save-as)

Save the currently selected document from the File Viewer as a new file

### Save All[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#save-all)

Save all currently open documents from the File Viewer

Project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#project)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------

### New Project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#new-project)

Create a new WolvenKit mod project

### Open Project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#open-project)

Open an existing WolvenKit mod project

### Project Configuration[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#project-configuration)

Configure various project settings

### Scan project for broken references[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#scan-project-for-broken-references)

This will go through every file in your project, checking if **referenced resources** are **in your project** or **part of the base game**.

If you are using a texture library, you will obviously get false positives here.

### Scan for unused files[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#scan-for-unused-files)

The opposite of "Scan project for broken references" — this will check every file in your project and check if it's used by something.

Will give you a list that you can copy to clipboard, or options to move/delete the files.

This feature is experimental, so handle with care!

### Delete empty folders[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#delete-empty-folders)

Will yeet folders without files in them. Works recursively (will also delete empty folder trees).

### Run File Validation on entire project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#run-file-validation-on-entire-project)

Will run the [Wolvenkit File Validation](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/file-validation)
 on every file in your project, and open the log file afterwards. This will generate a lot of duplicate text, but is the best way to find mistakes.

Build[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#build)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Pack Project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#pack-project)

Generates the install-ready structure in your project's `packed` folder, then creates a zip file.

The generated zip file is ready for Nexus upload!

### Pack as REDmod[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#pack-as-redmod)

Generates the install-ready structure (REDmod format) in your project's `packed` folder, then creates a zip file.

This option is hidden by default and can be turned on in the [Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#settings)
. Read [here](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#why-would-i-want-this)
 why.

### Install[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install)

Runs [Pack Project](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#pack-project)
, then copies the contents of `packed` to your game directory. You can learn about [Archive files Load Order](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order)
 on the yellow wiki.

### Install as REDmod[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install-as-redmod)

Runs [Pack as REDmod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#pack-as-redmod)
, then copies the contents of `packed` to your game directory.

This option is hidden by default and can be turned on in the [Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#settings)
. Read [here](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#why-would-i-want-this)
 why.

### Install & Launch Game[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install-and-launch-game)

Runs [Install](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install)
, then launches the game for you

### Install as REDmod & Launch Game[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install-as-redmod-and-launch-game)

Runs [Install as REDmod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install-as-redmod)
, then launches the game for you

This option is hidden by default and can be turned on in the [Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#settings)
. Read [here](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#why-would-i-want-this)
 why.

### Clean All[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#clean-all)

Deletes the contents of your Wolvenkit project's `packed` directory.

### Hot Reload[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#hot-reload)

Pack archives and instantly install to game directory while Cyberpunk 2077 is running for testing (Requires [RedHotTools](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redhottools)
).

### Launch Profiles[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#launch-profiles)

Create and modify one-click pack & launch operations

View[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#view)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

### Project Explorer[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#project-explorer)

Toggles the [Project Explorer](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#project-explorer)
 on or off

### Asset Browser[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#asset-browser)

Toggles the [Asset Browser](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#asset-browser)
 on or off

### Properties[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#properties)

Toggles the [Properties](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/properties)
 panel for your currently selected file or off

### Log[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#log)

Toggles the [Log](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/log)
 panel on or off.

### Tweak Browser[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#tweak-browser)

Toggles the **Tweak Browser** on or off. This panel will let you browse the TweakDB.

### LocKey browser[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#lockey-browser)

Toggles the LocKey browser on or off. This panel will let you browse translation strings.

### Show File Preview[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#show-file-preview)

Same as [**Settings**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings#show-file-preview)
 option - enables/disables interactive Quick Previews within the Properties panel.

This feature impacts performance. Navigating the Asset Browser or Project Explorer will be **faster** if the preview is **toggled off** or **inactive**.

### Layout[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#layout)

#### Save Layout to Project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#save-layout-to-project)

Saves the [**Editor**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor)
 UI layout to the current WolvenKit project, making it persistent across sessions.

If you do this without an open Wolvenkit project, the view will be saved to the default file `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Roaming\REDModding\WolvenKit\DockStates.xml`

#### Reset Layout[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#reset-layout)

Resets the UI layout to the default from one of the following files (by priority):

1.  `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Roaming\REDModding\WolvenKit\DockStates.xml`
    
2.  `Path\To\Wolvenkit\DockStatesDefault.xml`
    

This can be especially helpful if the layout is bugged or an editor window is unrecoverable.

Tools[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#tools)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Depot Generator[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#depot-generator)

Tool for building a `depot`, a collection of extracted game assets to use them in other tools. [**Read more**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/tools#depot-generator)

### Sound Modding Tool[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#sound-modding-tool)

Tool for modding sound files [**Read more**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/tools#sound-modding-tool)

### **Script Manager**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#script-manager)

Toggles the [Script Manager](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#script-manager)

### Import Tool[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#import-tool)

Toggles the [Import Tool](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/tools/tools-import-export#import-tool)

### Export Tool[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#export-tool)

Toggles the [Export Tool](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/tools/tools-import-export#export-tool)

### Settings[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#settings)

Open the WolvenKit [**Settings**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/settings)
 page

Game[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#game)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

### Launch Game[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#launch-game)

Launches Cyberpunk 2077

### Launch Game with Steam[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#launch-game-with-steam)

Launches Cyberpunk 2077 using Steam

### Open Game Folder[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#open-game-folder)

Opens the Cyberpunk 2077 game directory in Windows Explorer

Extensions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#extensions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Plugin Manager[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#plugin-manager)

Opens the WolvenKit Plugin Manager

#### Mod Manager[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#mod-manager)

Opens the WolvenKit Plugin Manager

#### Cyberpunk Blender Add-on[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#cyberpunk-blender-add-on)

Opens external-link to the Cyberpunk Blender Add-on GitHub page [https://github.com/WolvenKit/Cyberpunk-Blender-add-on](https://github.com/WolvenKit/Cyberpunk-Blender-add-on)

Help[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#help)

----------------------------------------------------------------------

### Setting Up WolvenKit[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#setting-up-wolvenkit)

Opens an external-link to the **Setup** page

### Creating a Mod[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#creating-a-mod)

Opens an external-link to the [**Creating a Mod**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/creating-a-mod)
 page

#### Discord Invitation[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#discord-invitation)

### Opens an external-link to a [Discord](https://discord.gg/Epkq79kd96)
 invitation[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#opens-an-external-link-to-a-discord-invitation)

### About WolvenKit[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#about-wolvenkit)

Opens an external-link to the [**About**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/about)
 page

### Open Log Folder[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#open-log-folder)

Opens the folder with the Wolvenkit Logs

# Modding on macOS | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos.md)
.

This guide is currently a work in progress.

**Modding Support on MacOS (or Lack Thereof)**

At the time of writing, the only framework mod that is unofficially supported on the MacOS build of Cyberpunk 2077 is [redscript](https://wiki.redmodding.org/redscript/)
 starting with the 0.5.29 build. As such, the only mods that are supported on MacOS are pure **redscript-only** mods (i.e. no Codeware, no ArchiveXL, etc.)

Additionally, due to the security features built into MacOS, modding the game is possible, but much more annoying. You'll get a lot of scary-looking warnings and permission requests, but this is normal. Don't worry! We'll go through all of them here.

Getting Started[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#getting-started)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1

**Download redscript for macOS**

Find the latest release on the [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/jac3km4/redscript/releases/)
 for redscript

Be sure to download `redscript-vX.X.XX-macos.zip` , NOT `redscript-cli-aarch64-darwin` !

2

**Install redscript to your game directory**

Copy the contents of the zip/folder to your Cyberpunk 2077 install folder (the one with `Cyberpunk2077.app`). The default locations can be found here:

*   GOG: `/Applications/Cyberpunk 2077/`
    
*   Steam: `~/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/`
    
*   App Store: `/Applications/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/Data/`
    

3

**Unblock redscript in macOS**

Open the Terminal app and run the following command to tell MacOS redscript is safe to run:

*   GOG:
    

Copy

    xattr -r -d com.apple.quarantine "/Applications/Cyberpunk 2077/engine/tools/"

*   Steam:
    

Copy

    xattr -r -d com.apple.quarantine "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/engine/tools/"

*   App Store:
    

Copy

    xattr -r -d com.apple.quarantine "/Applications/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/Data/engine/tools/"

4

**Download input loader for macOS**

Find the latest release on the [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/risner/cyberpunk2077-input-loader-mac/releases/)
 for redscript

5

**Install input loader to your game directory**

Copy the contents of the zip/folder to your Cyberpunk 2077 install folder (the one with `Cyberpunk2077.app`). The default locations can be found here:

*   GOG: `/Applications/Cyberpunk 2077/`
    
*   Steam: `~/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/`
    
*   App Store: `/Applications/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/Data/`
    

6

**Make sure your script is executable**

*   GOG:
    

Copy

    chmod +x "/Applications/Cyberpunk 2077/launch_modded.sh"

*   Steam:
    

Copy

    chmod +x "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/launch_modded.sh"

*   App Store:
    

Copy

    chmod +x "/Applications/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/Data/launch_modded.sh"

Running Cyberpunk 2077 on macOS with redscript[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#running-cyberpunk-2077-on-macos-with-redscript)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The MacOS build of Cyberpunk 2077 will not start redscript automatically like the Windows build. Therefore, any redscript mods you install will not be included in the game automatically, but you don't have to run the script every time, only with new mods or updates **to let them compile**. You have three options:

### 1\. Link bash script to your game client[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#id-1.-link-bash-script-to-your-game-client)

_a) GOG:_

1.  Launch GOG Galaxy → **Cyberpunk 2077 → More → Manage Installation → Configure → Add another executable / arguments**
    
2.  Locate the game path and ensure to select the script
    
    Copy
    
        /Applications/Cyberpunk 2077/launch_modded.sh
    
3.  Press **Select an executable to link,** ensure **Default executable** is **checked** and press **OK**.
    

Now GOG Galaxy will run your script whenever you press **Play**.

_b) Steam:_

Steam does not let you choose a different executable on macOS, so the workaround is to temporarily replace the top-level launcher with the script.

1.  Rename the executable inside `Cyberpunk2077.app` :
    

1.  Using your preferred text editor (use `TextEdit` if you do not have another one), update `launch_modded.sh` so it uses a fixed macOS path, changes into the game directory before running the tools, and launches the renamed executable:
    

Save the file with `Command` + `S`.

1.  Rename the launch script to executable:
    

1.  Make sure it's executable:
    

If you want to undo this later, restore the original launcher and put the script back:

_c) App Store_

Some values within `launch_modded.sh` will need to be edited for it to launch the game correctly.

1.  Adjust `launch_modded.sh` to these changes using preferable text editor (use `Text Edit`, if you don't have any):
    

*   Locate following lines:
    

*   Replace it with following:
    

1.  Make sure it's executable:
    

Be sure the script opens with Terminal by default, right click `launch_modded.sh` → Show info → Open with: → Select 'Terminal'

1.  _(Optional)_ Create a shortcut to open more easily
    

### 2\. Run the Included Bash Script to Start Cyberpunk 2077[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#id-2.-run-the-included-bash-script-to-start-cyberpunk-2077)

*   From Finder, double click the `launch_modded.sh` file in the `Cyberpunk 2077` folder
    

or

*   From Terminal, `cd` to your Cyberpunk 2077 folder (the same as before) and run `./launch_modded.sh`
    

### 3\. Manually run redscript, then launch the game normally[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#id-3.-manually-run-redscript-then-launch-the-game-normally)

😏 (more to come)

[PreviousModding on Linux](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux)
[NextTroubleshooting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/user-guide-troubleshooting)

Last updated 3 months ago

*   [Getting Started](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#getting-started)
    
*   [Running Cyberpunk 2077 on macOS with redscript](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#running-cyberpunk-2077-on-macos-with-redscript)
    
*   [1\. Link bash script to your game client](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#id-1.-link-bash-script-to-your-game-client)
    
*   [2\. Run the Included Bash Script to Start Cyberpunk 2077](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#id-2.-run-the-included-bash-script-to-start-cyberpunk-2077)
    
*   [3\. Manually run redscript, then launch the game normally](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-macos#id-3.-manually-run-redscript-then-launch-the-game-normally)
    

Copy

    mv "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/MacOS/Cyberpunk2077" \
       "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/MacOS/Cyberpunk2077_real"

Copy

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    game_dir="$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077"
    
    cd "$game_dir" || exit 1
    
    "$game_dir/engine/tools/scc" -compile "$game_dir/r6/scripts"
    perl "$game_dir/engine/tools/inputloader.pl"
    exec "$game_dir/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/MacOS/Cyberpunk2077_real" "$@"

Copy

    mv "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/launch_modded.sh" \
       "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077"

Copy

    chmod +x "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077"

Copy

    mv "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/MacOS/Cyberpunk2077_real" \
       "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/MacOS/Cyberpunk2077"
    
    mv "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/Cyberpunk2077" \
       "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Steam/steamapps/common/Cyberpunk 2077/launch_modded.sh"

Copy

    "$game_dir/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/MacOS/Cyberpunk2077"

Copy

    "$game_dir/../MacOS/Cyberpunk2077"

Copy

    chmod +x "/Applications/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/Data/launch_modded.sh"

Copy

    ln -s "/Applications/Cyberpunk2077.app/Contents/Data/launch_modded.sh" "/Applications/Cyberpunk 2077 Modded"

# Tweaks | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks.md)
.

**Summary**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks#summary)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Created:** Mar 18 2024 by @Bioskop **Last documented update:** Mar 24 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)

This is the landing page for the Tweaks section of the wiki. It doesn't contain any information on its own, but you can explore its subcategories in the menu bar or use the links at the bottom of the page to browse them.

### Tweak modding guides[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks#tweak-modding-guides)

Check [Tweak modding: Guides](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks/tweak-modding-guides)
 for a collection of guides on this topic

* * *

TweakDB[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks#tweakdb)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TweakDB is a database from REDengine 4 designed for readonly in-game access. It contains static information about characters, items, AI behavior, etc.

#### For further documentation on TweakDB enter to Psiberx github:[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks#for-further-documentation-on-tweakdb-enter-to-psiberx-github)

[![Logo](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2Ffluidicon.png&width=20&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a9296982&sv=2)TweakDBGitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-tweak-xl/wiki/TweakDB)

TweakXL:[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks#tweakxl)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TweakXL is a modding tool and a framework to create mods that modify TweakDB, a proprietary database of REDengine 4, containing essential information about game entities and behavior.

#### For further documentation on TweakXL enter to Psiberx github:[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks#for-further-documentation-on-tweakxl-enter-to-psiberx-github)

[![Logo](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2Ffluidicon.png&width=20&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a9296982&sv=2)GitHub - psiberx/cp2077-tweak-xl: Cyberpunk 2077 mod that allows you to modify TweakDB.GitHub](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-tweak-xl)

[PreviousUV Mapping: texturing a 3d object](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/uv-mapping-texturing-a-3d-object)
[NextTweakDB: Game database](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks/tweaks)

# Archive files Load Order | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#summary)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Written & Published**: Nov 22, 2023 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)

**Last documented update:** January 20th, 2026 by [minnierylands](mailto:undefined)

This page will explain to you what Load Order is and how it works, then tell you how to influence it.

TL;DR[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#tl-dr)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You're not supposed to manage load order conflicts. If you ever feel the need to do that, a modder fucked up.

In that case you may still influence`.archive` mods and REDmods. **Tweak mods** may conflict as well since tweakDB is a database of static values (e.g. two mods both edit the weapon stats of the Masamune).

*   For REDmods, see [Conflicts and Load Order](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#conflicts-and-load-order)
    
*   For .archive mods, rename files in ASCII alphabetical order. Do not use any [tools](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/11126)
     **other than your mod manager** to change mod load order, as they create more problems than they solve.
    
*   There currently is no tool to check tweak conflicts
    
*   Check [Load order and Wolvenkit](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-and-wolvenkit)
     if you are making a mod
    

What is Load Order?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#what-is-load-order)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Unless two mods are changing the same file, you don't need to bother about this at all. Unfortunately, the moment you're using frameworks or anything body modding related, you _will_ run into this scenario.

In Cyberpunk, conflicts inside `.archive` mods are one per-file basis, whatever mod modifies a file **first** will win – unlike e.g. Skyrim, where esps may overwrite each other.

#### Example[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#example)

There are two mods that change boob size (and both mods modify the same file, e.g. a rig or mesh):

Since **i\_know\_nothing\_about\_back\_problems** will load first, the conflicting file inside **realistic\_boobs\_DDDDDDDDD\_cup** does nothing.

How are mods ordered?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#how-are-mods-ordered)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The game loads mods in the following order (higher wins):

### 1\. .archive mods[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#id-1.-.archive-mods)

All .archive files in `Cyberpunk 2077/archive/pc/mod` in [ASCII-alphabetical order](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-ascii-sort)
.

Example: To load the conflicting file from **realistic\_boobs\_DDDDDDDDD\_cup.archive** before **i\_know\_nothing\_about\_back\_problems.archive** you could simply change the capitalisation. This will lead to the following order

If you change the load order by renaming a file, it is suggested to indicate said change in your mod manager, for example by also renaming the mod in the manager to reflect the change made. In this case if the mod is named "**Backbreaker Boobs"**, you could change it's name in the manager to **"Backbreaker Boobs - renamed to load before Backpain is a myth"**. This helps keep an overview about changed file names and load order and minimises confusion later on when adding more mods.

**Note: If a file called** `**modlist.txt**` **is found inside** `**archive/pc/mod**` **then the game will load archives according to the order inside that file. Delete this file and save yourself from a world of pain.**

**If you really need to change where a mod loads, change the name of the file.**

modlist.txt example[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#modlist.txt-example)

The file may contain archive names, one mod archive name in each line. Example:

This means the game will load `modb` before `moda`, even though the filenames are not in alphabetical order. This allows for conflict management without renaming the physical files.

### 2\. REDmods[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#id-2.-redmods)

Folders in `Cyberpunk 2077/mods` are loaded strictly **after** `/archive/pc/mod` in [ASCII-alphabetical order](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-ascii-sort)
.

### Load order: ASCII sort[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-ascii-sort)

The game reads mods in ASCII-alphabetical order (REDmods by **folder name**, .archive files by **archive file name**).

This is ASCII ordering:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffiles.gitbook.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Fuploads%252FJ7IAaw7SEdYkWNCWeT4V%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dacfd4e9c-7c2c-4a11-89fc-6bbef5211eec&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=873b8fd3&sv=2)

Source: [https://www.academia.edu/19644191](https://www.academia.edu/19644191)

#### Example[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#example-1)

Mod name

Pos

why

MyMod.archive

2

M comes before m

myMod.archive

3

!myMod.archive

1

! comes before A

How to use this?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#how-to-use-this)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can re-name your folders and .archive files following the rules under [Load order: ASCII sort](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-ascii-sort)
 to influence in which order the game will load your mods.

Prefixing your archive file names with non-alphanumeric characters works fine in case of Cyberpunk, but is a terrible habit that might break file paths in other games or operating systems.

You will forget the warning above and re-learn this the hard way.

Load order and Wolvenkit[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-and-wolvenkit)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

By default, [installing a mod with Wolvenkit](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu/toolbar#install)
 will create an [archive mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#id-1.-.archive-mods)
 which is subject to the rules above (see [Load order: ASCII sort](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#load-order-ascii-sort)
). Unless you specifically need to **overwrite other modded files**, you can completely ignore this.

To re-name your packed files, use Project -> Configure (check also: [Project naming and mod load order](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/wolvenkit-projects#project-naming-and-mod-load-order)
)

### More Examples[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#more-examples)

If you are creating a **compatibility mod** (something that modifies the files of another installed mod), then yours needs to load **first**.

For example, if you want to do a custom recolour of the mod [`_ArchiveXL_Netrunner_Variants.archive`](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/recolours-and-refits)
, then your Wolvenkit project could be named `_00_ArchiveXL_Netrunner_Variants`, or `_ArchiveXL_Netrunner_00_Recolour`.

[PreviousVortex Mod Manager](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started/vortex-mod-manager)
[NextREDmod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod)

Last updated 6 months ago

*   [Summary](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/load-order#summary)

# REDMod: Usage | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#summary)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

REDmod is a command line tool developed by CDPR and Yigsoft which can be used to stage installed mods to work with a modded game.

### Wait, this is not what I want![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#wait-this-is-not-what-i-want)

This section is for managing mods with REDmod.

*   For install instructions, check [Using Mods](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077)
    
*   For creating mods, see the **Modding Guides** section in the wiki's navigation tree to the left.
    

What does REDmod do for me?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#what-does-redmod-do-for-me)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

REDmod **preloads** any mods on its list, loading them as if they were a part of the game's regular structure. Preloading requires the game to be started via launcher (or Vortex) and will add a short delay, but the loading times during gameplay will be much faster.

### That sounds awesome, why is that not the default?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#that-sounds-awesome-why-is-that-not-the-default)

*   Compatibility: Not all mods can be easily converted. Some outright break in the process.
    
*   Especially modders don't like the extra delay during startup
    
*   While the mods are precompiled, a console window will be visible. This scares many users.
    

The only thing that **requires** REDmod format are custom audio file additions (or use the [Audioware](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/12001)
 mod from Nexus).

You can **convert** REDmods to the legacy format by putting any files into the [correct subfolders](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077)
. `.script` files need to be converted to `.reds`, but TweakXL can simply read REDMod tweaks.

Using Mods[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#using-mods)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The game loads REDmods from this path: `<`[`Cyberpunk 2077`](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
`>/mods/<name>`.

> EXAMPLE: REDMod: `C:\GoG\`[`Cyberpunk 2077`](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
> `\mods\<name>\archives\<name>.archive` Legacy: `C:\GoG\`[`Cyberpunk 2077`](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
> `\archive\PC\mod\<name>.archive`

Legacy archive mods in [`<Cyberpunk 2077>`](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
`/archive/pc/mod` are still supported, but their load order cannot be managed with REDmod.

Legacy mods will load **before** any REDmods.

Before being able to play a modded game, you need to [**deploy**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#how-do-i-deploy)
 your installed mods.

Then, you need to [activate mods](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#activating-mods)
 before starting the game.

#### How do I deploy?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#how-do-i-deploy)

You can handle deployment and load order with

*   the **REDlauncher** that comes with the game
    
*   **Vortex** Mod Manager from NexusMods
    
*   REDmod WolvenKit integration and manage your mods easily from within the **WolvenKit Mod Manager**
    
*   starting Cyberpunk with the parameter `-modded`
    

#### What is deployed?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#what-is-deployed)

Scripts: Compiled into a script blob

[<Cyberpunk 2077>](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
/mods//scripts

Tweaks: Compiled into a modded tweakDB

[<Cyberpunk 2077>](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
/mods//tweaks

Sound files

[<Cyberpunk 2077>](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
/mods//customSounds

Activating mods[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#activating-mods)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### **GOG, Steam** and **Epic**[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#gog-steam-and-epic)

*   Navigate to _Cyberpunk 2077_
    
*   Click **Play**
    
*   Click the **Settings** button and **Enable mods**
    
*   Click **Play** (Do not skip this)
    
*   A window will open showing progress on mod deployment.
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-8c755ebc06c02f75bbc1be09fb7a3366edefdfcc%252Fgog_prelauncher.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D22c2f222-927d-4b2c-b537-7e057ed23425&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5b980687&sv=2)

Conflicts and Load Order[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#conflicts-and-load-order)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Conflicts are handled on a per-file basis, but can be handled by specifying a specific _load order_. The rules here are the opposite of e.g. Skyrim: **the first mod** to change a file wins.

**Example:**

**modA** contains a script file `Cyberpunk 2077\mods\modA\scripts\cyberpunk\player\player.script`. **modB** contains a script file with the same name: `Cyberpunk 2077\mods\modB\scripts\cyberpunk\player\player.script`

This will discard all changes **modB** made, since by default, REDmod and the game loads mods alphabetically (modA overrides changes in modB).

However, you can specify a load order with REDmod, which the game will respect.

Troubleshooting[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#troubleshooting)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If a REDmod is giving you trouble, you can force a redeploy by either of the following means:

1.  Either
    
    1.  Delete the content of `r6/cache/modded` in your Cyberpunk directory
        
    
2.  or
    
    1.  Uninstall the mod
        
    2.  Start the game
        
    3.  As soon as it launches, close the game again
        
    4.  Reinstall the mod
        
    
3.  Now, restart the game. REDMod will rebuild the cache and hopefully fix your problem.
    

If that doesn't do it, you can consult the [troubleshooting guide](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#troubleshooting)
 for advice.

Advanced Topics[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#advanced-topics)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Deploying Mods - Automatically[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#deploying-mods-automatically)

REDMod's redeploy is triggered on any of these changes:

*   **mods change**: You added or removed a mod
    
*   A mod's **version** changes: The version number in the `info.json` was changed.
    
*   A mod's **files** change: You added or removed archives
    

### Deploying Mods - Manually[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#deploying-mods-manually)

The REDmod `deploy` command stages installed mods to work when the game starts with the `modded` flag.

### Deploying Mods - WolvenKit Integration[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#deploying-mods-wolvenkit-integration)

*   Open the WolvenKit Mod Manager.
    
*   Click the _Deploy Mods_ button in the toolbar.
    

You will see all installed mods in your game. Enable the mods you would like to play with.

### Handling Conflicts - Manually[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#handling-conflicts-manually)

You can specify a specific load order with the **\-mod** parameter:

This will override changes to `\cyberpunk\player\player.script` that **modA** makes in favor of **modB**. It will also tell the game to load **modB** before **modA** and **modC**.

### Handling Conflicts - WolvenKit integration[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#handling-conflicts-wolvenkit-integration)

*   Open the WolvenKit Mod Manager.
    
*   Adjust the load order by dragging and dropping rows.
    
*   Click the _Deploy Mods_ button.
    

You can adjust the load order by dragging and dropping the installed mods inside the mod manager.

When you click the _Deploy Mods_ button, the current load order will be forwarded to redmod.exe and mods will be deployed and loaded in this order.

### Starting a Modded Game - Manually[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod/usage#starting-a-modded-game-manually)

To start a modded game that loads mods deployed by REDmod use the `-modded` flag on **Cyberpunk2077.exe**

**Example:**

This however will skip the initialization process that should be done after any new REDmod mod install.

> INFO The executable is located at `<Cyberpunk 2077>\bin\x64\Cyberpunk2077.exe`

# Assets explained | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets.md)
.

What is an asset?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#what-is-an-asset)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

An asset is a specific, objectified concept in the game that can be highly detailed like a blade of grass, or larger like a building, or intangible like sound effects. Unity defines an asset as, "Shorthand for anything that goes into a video game – characters, objects, sound effects, maps, environments, etc."

Every asset is defined in a set of [normalized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_normalization)
 asset files that describe its relationship to the game, other assets, and itself.

Visual Asset-Related File Formats[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#visual-asset-related-file-formats)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The following list contains file formats that are used to compose visual assets such as vehicles and weapons.

The community-devloped tool **WolvenKit** can be used to browse game archives and extract game files. [Learn more](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/)

### MESH[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#mesh)

3d models

### XBM (images and Textures)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#xbm-images-and-textures)

2d textures mapped onto meshes, advertisements, or any other game asset that uses an image in the game.

### APP (appearance)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#app-appearance)

Sets up appearances for assets such as NPCs and vehicles referenced by [**entity files**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#ent-entity)

### BK2 (Videos)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#bk2-videos)

[**Bink 2**](https://www.radgametools.com/bnkdown.htm)
 file format (.bk2) that us used for any videos in game (Televsion, quest overlays, or some billboards)

### ENT (entity)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#ent-entity)

Parent file for most visual assets which is directly linked by the [**Tweak DB**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/tweaks/tweaks#what-is-the-tweak-db)

### HP (hair profile)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#hp-hair-profile)

Color ramp data to set colors for meshes using the hair [**shader**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/shaders)

### MI (material instance)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#mi-material-instance)

Instanced shaders for mesh materials

### MLMASK (multilayered mask)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#mlmask-multilayered-mask)

Mask component of the [**Multilayer supershader**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered)

### MLSETUP (multilayered setup)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#mlsetup-multilayered-setup)

Material setup component of the [**Multilayer supershader**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered)

### MLTEMPLATE (multilayered template)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#mltemplate-multilayered-template)

Pre-defined tiling surface component of the [**Multilayer supershader**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered)

### MT (material template)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#mt-material-template)

Base shader files for REDengine

* * *

Visual Asset Graphs[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#visual-asset-graphs)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

#### Vehicles[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/assets#vehicles)

# Getting Started | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/native-interactions-framework/getting-started.md)
.

Creating Project[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/native-interactions-framework/getting-started#creating-project)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   All interactions are organized intro projects, you usually have one project per mod
    
*   To create a new project, go to the `Projects` tab
    
*   Enter a name, then hit the save icon to create and load a new project
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-dbe56f31a59d8ff33777b7bbcd9e3bed69719313%252FNIFProjects.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b9dd334b&sv=2)

Creating, managing and editing existing projects

In this guide, "loading" a project means loading it to edit, as any project installed will be active by default

Adding Interactions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/native-interactions-framework/getting-started#adding-interactions)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   To add an interaction, make sure you have loaded a project by pressing the "Load" icon to the left of it's name in the `Projects` tab
    
*   Switch to the `Edit Project` Tab
    
*   Now select the type of interaction from the dropdown, add a name, and hit the plus icon to add it
    
    *   New interactions will be placed at the player's feet
        
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-1a99c525f3ef2420bf5172aa4a9e1a3f665ed86b%252FNIFAddNew.gif%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=80f04b28&sv=2)

Add a new interaction

Edit Interactions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/native-interactions-framework/getting-started#edit-interactions)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Information on how to edit an interaction and what it's properties do, can be found here:
    
    *   [Interaction Settings](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/native-interactions-framework/interaction-settings)
        
    

Sharing Creations[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/native-interactions-framework/getting-started#sharing-creations)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   For each project, an equally named `.json` file is created in the mod's folder:
    
*   `Cyberpunk 2077\bin\x64\plugins\cyber_engine_tweaks\mods\nativeInteractions\projects\projectName.json`
    
*   To publish your mod, simply include the project's JSON file, and add NIF as requirement
    

Project files for sharing can be found in

`Cyberpunk 2077\bin\x64\plugins\cyber_engine_tweaks\mods\nativeInteractions\projects`

# World Editing | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing.md)
.

This page will introduce you to the very basic structure of the world and the necessary tools to effectively modify the surroundings to your liking.

### World Structure[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing#world-structure)

The world of Cyberpunk2077 consists of [nodes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/reference-world-sectors/reference-.streamingsector-node-types)
, which can represent anything from visible meshes, collision, lights, effects to sounds, areas dictating where NPCs can spawn and more. These are grouped together into `streamingsector`'s which are packed inside the archive files of the game. Thus, directly editing them is not possible without causing conflicts (This is relevant for deleting and modifying nodes). To avoid that, ArchiveXL allows working with streamingsectors in a non-destructible way.

Additions to the world are also made possible by ArchiveXL, which can tell the game to load custom streamingsectors.

More detailed information can be found here:

[The whole world: .streamingsectors](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/the-whole-world-.streamingsector)

[Sectors of Interest](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/reference-world-sectors)

[Node Types](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/reference-world-sectors/reference-.streamingsector-node-types)

[Mod Load Order](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/wolvenkit-projects#project-naming-and-mod-load-order)

### Tools You Will Need[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing#tools-you-will-need)

*   **ArchiveXL (**[**Nexus**](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/4198)
    **|**[**Wiki**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/core-mods-explained/archivexl)
    **):** In the context of world editing, ArchiveXL allows for the non-destructive modification of sector files (E.g. Removing or modifying nodes), and also handles the merging of custom streamingsectors into the game.
    
*   **RedHotTools (**[**GitHub**](https://github.com/psiberx/cp2077-red-hot-tools)
    **|**[**Wiki**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redhottools)
    **):** Allows for inspection of nodes and objects at runtime, providing you details like nodetype, nodeinstance, the sectorpath and more.
    

Instructions on how to find locations using RHT can be found [here](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/reference-world-sectors/places)
.

*   **World Builder** **(**[**GitHub**](https://github.com/justarandomguyintheinternet/CP77_entSpawner)
    **|**[**Nexus**](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/20660)
    **|**[**Wiki**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/object-spawner)
    **):** Main tool for adding new nodes to the game-world. Supports dozens of node types and has access to all game resources, including AMM props. Mods made using it are native world edits, using ArchiveXL to make the game load the custom sectors.
    

A fork of World Builder with extended features that aren't yet in the main repository is available exclusively on [GitHub](https://github.com/Akiway/CP77_entSpawner)
.

*   **UnlimitedGeometryCacheStreaming** ([Nexus](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/29096)
    |[GitHub](https://github.com/notaspirit/UnlimitedGeometryCacheStreaming)
    ): Allows streaming sectors to use any collision mesh entry anywhere, is required for the finished mod as well if it contains collision meshes.
    
*   **removalEditor (**[**GitHub**](https://github.com/justarandomguyintheinternet/CP77_removalEditor)
    **):** Integrates with RedHotTools to allow easily creating node removal files for ArchiveXL.
    
*   **VolumetricSelection2077 (**[**GitHub**](https://github.com/notaspirit/VolumetricSelection2077)
    **):** Quickly and easily remove parts of the world using an in game selection box.
    
*   **Wolvenkit (**[**GitHub**](https://github.com/WolvenKit/WolvenKit)
    **|**[**Wiki**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit)
    **):** Comprehensive mod creator, allows finding more detailed information about nodes e.g. finding their actors. Includes adding custom streamingsectors from entSpawner, adding resources like custom meshes and much more.
    
*   **Blender (**[**Site**](https://www.blender.org/download/)
    **|**[**Wiki**](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling/blender-getting-started)
    **):** 3D modeling software that enables creating custom assets that aren't already in the game.
    
*   **FreeFly (**[**Nexus**](https://www.nexusmods.com/cyberpunk2077/mods/780)
    **|**[**GitHub**](https://github.com/justarandomguyintheinternet/CP77_FreeFly)
    **):** Enables quick and easy traversal of the map and getting to places you normally wouldn't be able to, includes noclip.
    
*   All mods that these mods require to function.
    
*   It is highly recommended to join the [redmodding discord](https://discord.gg/redmodding)
     to stay up to date on new techniques and tools.
    

[PreviousinkWidgets On Vehicles](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/vehicles/inkwidgets-on-vehicles)
[NextWorld Builder](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing/object-spawner)
