# REDmod for mod devs | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#summary)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Last documented edit:** Sep 07 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)

This page tells you how to use REDmod for **mod creation**.

### Wait, that's not what I want![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#wait-thats-not-what-i-want)

*   If you want to know what REDmod does, check [here](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod#if-i-use-it-what-do-i-gain)
    
*   If you're a mod user, check the [REDmod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/redmod)
     page in the Mod Users section
    

What is REDmod? (Developer version)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#what-is-redmod-developer-version)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

REDmod is a command line tool developed by CDPR and Yigsoft, which allows us to interact with game files in a variety of ways.

Modules for mod creators:

Copy

    resource-export  - Export REDengine files to raw format for editing
    resource-import  - Import raw files to REDengine formats for use in your mod
    animation-import - import .re animation files to an animset
    util-hash        - Hashes paths/string with given hash algorithm
    help             - Displays helpful info about the other avaliable modules (use -h or -help on any command)

Folder Structure[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#folder-structure)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When creating a REDmod, there is a certain file structure that you need to follow to ensure that it can be used by others. You will want to have a folder that is the name of the mod and in this folder you will have a file named `info.json.` Whether you are doing [Animation Modding](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#animation-modding)
, [Script Modding](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#script-modding)
, [TweakDB Modding](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#tweakdb-modding)
, [Audio Modding](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#audio-modding)
, or other mods (archive mods), you will also need to have at least _one_ subfolder for the mod files to go into. This subfolder should be located on the same level as the `info.json` file, and be nested directly inside the folder with the mod name.

An example REDmod might look something like this:

When sharing you mod, you would package the folder that is the name of the mod. You could also package the mods folder with the "mod folder", but it is not necessary and it is assumed one might have other mods in their `mods` folder anyways.

### info.json[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#info.json)

For a REDmod to be recognized and loaded by the game engine, you must include an `info.json` file. This file sits in the root folder of the mod's folder at the same level as the subfolders containing your modded files. There are two required entries and a few optional entries that can be in this json file. At minimum a `name` field and a `version` field are needed:

In the above:

*   The `name` field is the mod's name and should generally be the same as the folder the `info.json` file sits in.
    
*   The `version` field is the mod's version. The version should generally use "Semantic Versioning"
    
*   The `description` field is optional for _all_ mods, but it is generally suggested one should include it so other tools have something to display for a given mod.
    
*   The `customSounds` field is semi-optional. It _is_ required for [Audio Modding](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod#audio-modding)
    .
    

> INFO - Semantic Versioning is a method of versioning where each number gives meaning for a release based on their position. It uses the format `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` where a `MAJOR` release change is one which will break workflows, `MINOR` could break a workflow but is generally safe and might introduce deprecations, and a `PATCH` is a small non breaking change that all users on the same `MAJOR` and `MINOR` can update to without any issues.

[PreviousRHT: Hot Reload](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redhottools/rht-hot-reload)
[NextExporting Resources to Raw Formats](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redmod/exporting-resources-to-raw-formats)

Last updated 1 year ago

Copy

     * root_folder
     * | -📁 mods
     * | |-📁 <Mod Name>
     * | | |-📁 archives
     * | | |-📁 scripts
     * | | |-📁 tweaks
     * | | |-📁 customSounds
     * | | |-📄 info.json

Copy

    {
        "name": "MODNAME",
        "description": "Description for the mod.",
        "version": "1.0.0",
        "customSounds":    [ ]
    }

# Getting Started: Installing Cyberpunk | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#summary)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Last documented update:** January 19th, 2026 by [minnierylands](mailto:undefined)

While it is _possible_ to install mods by hand, we recommend using a mod manager.

This guide will walk you through installing and configuring your game. If you have already done that, you can configure the mod manager of your choice:

[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started/vortex-mod-manager)

![Cover](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252FmB1FyJx4AUGHceEszzYp%252Fvortex-logomark.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbf7348c2-bed3-440f-8489-16ee3bca3b6c&width=752&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=59bc854&sv=2)

**Vortex**

[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started/mo2-mod-organizer-2)

![Cover](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252F49p7x4c6SrpcwwRiVuCn%252F37225415.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D52387763-e10c-4fba-9edd-99803bf0f60c&width=752&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5abcf608&sv=2)

**Mod Organizer 2**

To learn how to install mods, check [Using Mods](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077)

* * *

Prerequisites[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#prerequisites)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   You have **bought** Cyberpunk 2077 on the platform of your choice (Steam, GOG or EPIC)
    
*   You have the following things:
    
    *   [Visual C Redistributable 2022](https://aka.ms/vs/17/release/vc_redist.x64.exe)
        
    *   [.NET 6.0+](https://dotnet.microsoft.com/en-us/download/dotnet/6.0)
        
    
*   You have updated the following things:
    
    *   [Your graphics card](https://www.wikihow.com/Update-Your-Graphics-Driver)
         driver
        
    *   The latest [Windows Update](https://www.wikihow.com/Update-Windows)
         for your operating system (checkout [Modding on Linux](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux)
         if that's what you're doing)
        
    

* * *

Installing the Game[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#installing-the-game)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you already have the game installed, you can skip this section and proceed with [Configuring the Game](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#configuring-the-game)

In order to set up your game to be best suited for mods, you'll want to keep some things in mind:

*   Installing Cyberpunk 2077 on an SSD can speed up load time
    
*   Installing Cyberpunk 2077 outside of `Program Files` can stop Windows "protecting" your files
    

For the purpose of this guide, we will assume the following install paths:

Game install

`C:\Games\Cyberpunk 2077`

Mod download and staging

`C:\Games\Mods`

* * *

#### Permissions[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#permissions)

Folder permissions **can** cause problems down the line, especially for Windows users.

Most users never run into these, but you can prevent them altogether by installing your game to a folder like `C:\Games` rather than `C:\Program Files.`

By default, all three platforms (GOG, Steam, and Epic) want to install the game to their own install location, which defaults to `C:\Program Files`. Unfortunately, Windows is fussy about what it will permit there, which is not much.

This **can** cause problems in two different spots by "protecting" the Cyberpunk install directory from:

*   Modding tools such as mod managers (like our recommendation [Vortex](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started/vortex-mod-manager)
    )
    
*   CDPR's own REDmod launcher.
    

But hey, at least things are very secure.

You likely won't run into any of these problems, but you can rule them out altogether by installing Cyberpunk to a directory other than `C:\Program Files` or `C:\Program Files(x86)`, like `C:\Games\Cyberpunk 2077`

* * *

### Installation Guide[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#installation-guide)

Now we will go through the install options to use on each platform to accomplish this, and the recommended settings for modded Cyberpunk 2077.

Just choose the header below for which platform you're using!

Installing Through GOG

Installing Through Steam

Installing Through Epic

**Installing Cyberpunk: GOG**

If you already have the game installed, you can skip this section and proceed with [Configuring Cyberpunk](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#configuring-cyberpunk-gog)

1.  Open GOG Galaxy, click on "`Owned Games`" in the left sidebar. Scroll until you find Cyberpunk.
    

* * *

1.  Single click on its picture to display the main Cyberpunk page.
    

* * *

1.  Click the button at the top of the page labeled "`Install`".
    

* * *

1.  In the pop-up window, choose your install location:
    

*   Click the drop-down box
    
*   Select "`browse`"
    
*   Browse to the target folder (we're assuming `C:\Games`)
    

You don't need to create a subfolder for Cyberpunk, as GOG will do that for you.

* * *

1.  **Optional**: Select a game language other than English
    

* * *

1.  **Optional**: Create a desktop shortcut by ticking the first box "`Create desktop shortcut`"
    

* * *

1.  **Optional, but recommended**: Disable auto-updates by unchecking the second box "`Keep this game up to date automatically`"
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-6cff805baeb2bc4810d9468af23de1bb3534e74c%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=eee3e05&sv=2)

How the window should look for recommended install.

* * *

1.  Press "`Install`"
    

* * *

1.  Wait until GOG has done it's thing, then proceed to the next section.
    

**Installing Cyberpunk: Steam**

If you already have the game installed, you can skip this section and proceed with [Configuring Cyberpunk](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#configuring-cyberpunk-steam)

To be done

**Installing Cyberpunk: Epic**

If you already have the game installed, you can skip this section and proceed with [Configuring Cyberpunk](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#configuring-cyberpunk-epic)

To be done

* * *

Configuring the Game[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#configuring-the-game)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Great, now the game is installed!

To get the recommended settings, and to start modding your game, follow one of the guides below.

### Configuration Guide[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#configuration-guide)

Just choose the header below for which platform you're using!

Configuring Through GOG

Configuring Through Steam

Configuring Through Epic

**Configuring Cyberpunk: GOG**

Once the game has installed, we want to make it run with mods.

1.  Click the button to the right of the `**Play**` button (formerly the `install` button) pictured below.
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-be491c971dda5b349a5871790e138dbece7ba320%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a6cc6108&sv=2)

The Cyberpunk 2077 main page on GOG, with the sidebar open on the left

* * *

1.  From there, select "`**Manage Installation**`" and then "`**Configure**`". A window will pop up where you can change your desired settings:
    
    *   Installation (first tab): Disable auto-updates
        
    *   DLC (third tab): Select at least `**Cyberpunk 2077 REDMod**`
        
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-4c32867685398a1adf5ac797fd3d59f2060ca583%252FCP2077%2520Modding%2520Guide%2520GOG%2520Setup%2520Merged%2520Image.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f24c5fa9&sv=2)

You may select any additional settings you like, but these are the minimum recommended options.

* * *

1.  Click the `**OK**` button to return to the game's page.
    

* * *

1.  Click `**Play**` on the game's page
    

* * *

1.  Click the `**Settings**` button and **Enable mods**
    
    *   If you can't enable mods, it means REDmod is not installed. **Go back and repeat step 2**.
        
    

* * *

1.  Click `**Play**` (Do not skip this)
    

That's it, you are done with your install of the game! You can now move on to [Starting to mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#starting-to-mod)
!

**Configuring Cyberpunk: Steam**

1.  Navigate to _Cyberpunk 2077_
    

* * *

1.  Click `**Play**`
    

* * *

1.  Click the `**Settings**` button and **Enable mods**
    
    *   If you can't enable mods, that means **REDmod is not installed**. Check the game's DLCs and make sure to get it.
        
    

* * *

1.  Click `**Play**` (Do not skip this)
    

That's it, you are done with your install of the game! You can now move on to [Starting to mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#starting-to-mod)
!

**Configuring Cyberpunk: Epic**

1.  Navigate to _Cyberpunk 2077_
    

* * *

1.  Click `**Play**`
    

* * *

1.  Click the `**Settings**` button and **Enable mods**
    
    *   If you can't enable mods, that means **REDmod is not installed**. Check the game's DLCs and make sure to get it.
        
    

* * *

1.  Click `**Play**` (Do not skip this)
    

That's it, you are done with your install of the game! You can now move on to [Starting to mod](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#starting-to-mod)
!

* * *

Starting to mod[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started#starting-to-mod)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You now have the following options:

*   If you're not confident in modding your game, follow the extremely detailed guide under [Vortex Mod Manager](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/getting-started/vortex-mod-manager)
    
*   Otherwise, you can head to [Using Mods](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077)
     and start reading on how to do this

# Editing guidelines | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#summary)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Created:** Nov 10 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)
 **Last documented update:** Nov 10 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)

This page contains our wiki editing guidelines.

### Wait, this is not what I want![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#wait-this-is-not-what-i-want)

*   If you would rather learn how to edit the wiki, please check [The Wiki](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki)
     -> [How to edit](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki#how-to-edit)
    

Basics[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#basics)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 1\. Name your merge requests[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#id-1.-name-your-merge-requests)

The first thing you should do after clicking the "Edit in Change Request" button is to give your merge request a title. Not only will future you be grateful, nothing is more annoying than returning to something like this:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-396ab69632ee9683af59d1313fb3bf81e9029a0c%252Fwiki_name_your_merge_requests.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=58a24256&sv=2)

### 2\. Add a header on new pages[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#id-2.-add-a-header-on-new-pages)

Please give your wiki page a header (see [Summary](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#summary)
). You can find a template to copy and paste under [Page Header: Summary](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines/page-header-summary)
.

When updating a page, please adjust the "last documented update" line. (I keep forgetting myself, but it'll help people to see what is up-to-date and what isn't)

### 3\. Name your images[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#id-3.-name-your-images)

You can add images into the editor by either **pasting from clipboard** or **drag-and-dropping a file**.

Unfortunately, gitbook's default name is as helpful as the NCPD customer feedback box:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-39f7d5081b170bc158f4533e5d8cdb77d951379f%252Fwiki_image_names_suck.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=193c55ff&sv=2)

They [know about this](https://github.com/orgs/GitbookIO/discussions/691)
, but haven't changed it yet.

Please **name your images**. As of November 2024, you can find the "`Files`" section on the left, in a tab behind the "`Pages`" tree:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-5853c13872d38193d89e4e262476e018149ef15b%252Fwiki_rename_your_images.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=62354121&sv=2)

Styleguide[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#styleguide)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The internet is full of guides on writing nonfiction. [This one](https://slatestarcodex.com/2016/02/20/writing-advice/)
 is good.

### Content (what it is)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#content-what-it-is)

#### KISS: Keep it simple, stupid[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#kiss-keep-it-simple-stupid)

Keep guides **short** and **concise**.

Remember that most of the world are not native speakers of English. Keep your sentences short and use **clear language**.

You can use ChatGPT or [goblin.tools **Formalizer**](https://goblin.tools/Formalizer)
 (`More to the point (unwaffle))` to go over your guides before posting them.

1.  It's easier to follow a numbered list than to read a long paragraph
    
2.  Use **bold text** to highlight stuff that is important.
    
3.  For anything extra, check [Put extra info into expandables](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#put-extra-info-into-expandables)
    .
    

### Presentation (how it looks)[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#presentation-how-it-looks)

Your guides should be easy to read. Here's a few tips how to achieve that.

#### Put important stuff in boxes[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#put-important-stuff-in-boxes)

You probably read the box "Your guides should be easy to read..." first. That's why I put it in a box: I wanted you to!

#### Use headings to split content[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#use-headings-to-split-content)

You can **link** to headings, which makes them quite useful to split up long guides into manageable content. Here's how I (manavortex) use them:

*   **Heading 1** marks the beginning of a section
    
*   **Heading 2** marks the beginning of a subsection within a section
    
*   **Heading 3** splits subsections into individual parts
    

#### Put extra info into expandables[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#put-extra-info-into-expandables)

Anything that's not immediately relevant to the guide (but too interesting to cut out) can go into an **expendable box**.

By the way...[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#by-the-way)

Nobody likes walls of text. By this point most people know that you should have short, sweet paragraphs with line breaks between them. The shorter, the better. If you’re ever debating whether or not to end the paragraph and add a line break, err on the side of “yes”.

Once you understand this principle, you can generalize it to other aspects of your writing. For example, I stole the Last Psychiatrist’s style of section breaks – bold headers saying I., II., III., etc. Now instead of just paragraph breaks, you have two forms of break – paragraph break and section break. On some of my longest posts, including the [Anti-Reactionary FAQ](https://slatestarcodex.com/2013/10/20/the-anti-reactionary-faq/)
 and [Meditations on Moloch](https://slatestarcodex.com/2014/07/30/meditations-on-moloch/)
, I add a _third_ level of break – in the first case, a supersection level in large fonts, in the latter, a subsection level with an underlined First, Second, etc. Again, if you’re ever debating more versus fewer breaks, err on the side of “more”.

Finishing a paragraph or section gives people a micro-burst of accomplishment and reward. It helps them chunk the basic insight together and remember it for later. You want people to be going – “okay, insight, good, another insight, good, another insight, good” and then eventually you can tie all of the insights together into a high-level insight. Then you can start over, until eventually at the end you tie all of the high-level insights together. It’s nice and structured and easy to work with. If they’re just following a winding stream of thought wherever it’s going, it’ll take a lot more mental work and they’ll get bored and wander off.

Remember that clickbait comes from big media corporations optimizing for easy readability, and that the epitome of clickbait is the listicle. But the insight of the listicle applies even to much more sophisticated intellectual pieces – people are much happier to read a long thing if they can be tricked into thinking it’s a series of small things.

[Source](https://slatestarcodex.com/2016/02/20/writing-advice/)

#### Use images for clarity[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/the-wiki/editing-guidelines#use-images-for-clarity)

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-faf425b65a6af437bf66ced205a14fc4f238534f%252Fwiki_sun_tzu_meme.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e86f831b&sv=2)

The right image can sum up several paragraphs of writing. Make sure to use the right images.

If you include screenshots of anything, make sure to **highlight** the **important parts** (e.g. by drawing boxes and arrows). You can use any graphic editing program (such as [Greenshot](https://getgreenshot.org/downloads/)
) for this.

# Textures, Materials and Shaders | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials.md)
.

This is the landing page for **materials** in Cyberpunk 2077. It contains a definition and a rough overview.

Check the section's sub pages in the wiki's navigation tree for more detailed information about the different kinds of materials. Additional material-related information can be found here:

#### General information[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#general-information)

*   Handy list of references: [Cheat sheet: Materials](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/cheat-sheet-materials)
    
*   Re-using materials as templates: [Re-using materials: .mi](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/file-formats/materials/re-using-materials-.mi)
    
*   How it works in the example: [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files)
    

#### Multilayered[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#multilayered)

*   General information: [Multilayered: Cyberpunk's supershader](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered)
    
*   Properties and definition: [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/broken-reference/README.md](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/broken-reference/README.md)
    
*   Preview images: [Multilayered: Previews](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered/multilayered-previews)
    

#### Hands-on:[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#hands-on)

*   [Changing materials, colors and textures](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/editing-existing-items/changing-materials-colors-and-textures)
     (guide)
    
*   [Self-made normal maps](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/self-made-normal-maps)
    
*   [Blending materials for more gooder colours](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered/blending-materials-for-more-gooder-colours)
    

Definition: Shader[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#definition-shader)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

In its original state, a [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files)
 is a collection of **vertices** (pixels). The space between those vertices is filled by **faces**, which form the 3d object's surface.

The shader is the thing that goes on top of the surface, rendering a **material** such as glass, skin, concrete, steel…

Without a shader, objects would be invisible in the game, the surface simply being transparent. In Cyberpunk, a default shader will be assigned in such cases (such as debug\_coloring.mt).

In Cyberpunk 2077, REDEngine shaders are implemented as `.mt`/`.remt` files.

### How can I use shaders?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#how-can-i-use-shaders)

If you have ever messed with an item's materials, then you already have.

To use a shader, you create a **Material Instance**, where you can configure the **parameters**. Not every parameter of a shader can be configured: since skin always has subsurface scattering, it makes no sense to tweak this. But since skin color already varies dramatically if you don't throw exotic chrome into the mix, you can change them easily via tint.

Definition: Material[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#definition-material)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For details how materials are used on a mesh, see the [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files)
, or learn how materials can be defined in a mesh via [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files#materialinstance-the-local-material](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files#materialinstance-the-local-material)
 or loaded externally from

**In the context of Cyberpunk**, a material is the thing that lets the shader define the surface properties of a [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files)
. Typically, you create a local instance, which will then pull in a shader via `baseMaterial`.

The property `baseMaterial` can point at a `.mt` / `.remt` file directly, or to an intermediary `.mi` file:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-8f4f7d1d0e20fdcceee7eda51e6de32016158802%252Fmaterials_mt_and_mi.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=de5b4345&sv=2)

### The .mi file[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#the-.mi-file)

Each part of a mesh (submesh) can have a different material assigned.

Do you want to know more? ( =>[https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files#chunkmaterials](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators/files-and-what-they-do/3d-objects-.mesh-files#chunkmaterials)
)

For a hands-on guide and something to play around/experiment with, check [AMM: Textured items and Cyberpunk Materials using Custom Props](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/textured-items-and-cyberpunk-materials)
.

What kinds of material exist?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#what-kinds-of-material-exist)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Textured[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#textured)

If you have modded other games, you're used to PBR materials with a bunch of textures like **diffuse**, **normal,** **metalness** etc.

See [Cheat sheet: Materials](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/cheat-sheet-materials)
 -> [PBR material](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/cheat-sheet-materials#pbr-material)
 for a list.

To change a textured material, see [Changing materials, colors and textures](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/editing-existing-items/changing-materials-colors-and-textures)
 -> [Changing materials, colors and textures](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/editing-existing-items/changing-materials-colors-and-textures#step-2-finding-the-correct-appearance)

Did you know? You can make Cyberpunk's default textured material glow.

### Multilayered[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#multilayered-1)

This is specific to Cyberpunk, and it is incredibly cool. If you're used to textured materials, you're probably going to hate it at first, but it has incredible versatility and re-usability.

*   What is a multilayered material and how does [Multilayered: Cyberpunk's supershader](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/multilayered)
     work?
    
*   What are these [properties](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators-theory/materials/broken-reference/README.md)
    , how do I use them?
    
*   [Changing the colours](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/editing-existing-items/changing-materials-colors-and-textures#multilayered-material)
    
*   [Changing the mask](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/custom-multilayermasks)
    

### Decal[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#decal)

A decal material is applied to a decal (sub)mesh, which is located on the surface of another mesh like a sticker.

Check out our guide on [Adding decals](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/editing-existing-items/adding-decals)
 for further explanation and detail.

### Emissive[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#emissive)

There are various kinds of emissive materials. See [Cheat sheet: Materials](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/cheat-sheet-materials)
 -> [Emissive Materials](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/references-lists-and-overviews/cheat-sheet-materials#emissive-materials)
 for a list.

Material wireframes[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials#material-wireframes)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can make materials display as wireframes of their geometry by changing the following property in their .mt.

Please do not overwrite original .mt files, as this will effect everything in the entire game. Always create your own local copy!

# Modding on Linux | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#summary)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This page tells you how to **set the launch options** for Steam under Linux.

### Wait, this is not what I want![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#wait-this-is-not-what-i-want)

*   If you have trouble after an update and have exhausted [all the usual steps](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/user-guide-troubleshooting/users-troubleshooting-after-a-game-update)
    , double-check your [Launch Options](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#launch-options)
     (sometimes, Steam helpfully changes them to make you crash)
    
*   If you want to optimize Cyberpunk 2077 settings for your SteamDeck, find a guide on [/r/SteamDeck](https://www.reddit.com/r/SteamDeck/)
    
*   For a video about modding on mac, see [https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x0CDcBui2qE&t=20s](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x0CDcBui2qE&t=20s)
    

TL;DR[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#tl-dr)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You need to

*   Install `d3dcompiler_47`
    
*   Install `vcrun2022`
    
*   Adjust the Steam launch option to be: `WINEDLLOVERRIDES="winmm,version=n,b" %command%`
    

The guide below has been confirmed working for dozens of people on SteamDeck, various Linux distributions, and Mac OS. Follow it **by the letter** and it will work for you as well.

Installing the required tool[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#installing-the-required-tool)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To play games on Linux, you need various programs and libraries. On the steam deck, most are preinstalled, and all you need is a tool to configure them.

This guide has information about Protontricks (recommended) and Lutris (only here for the sake of completeness). If you use anything else, we can not support you.

### Protontricks: Setting up[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#protontricks-setting-up)

Make sure that you are on the most recent version of Protontricks. This guide will follow how to perform these steps on a SteamDeck, but other systems running KDE Plasma should be similar. Other Linux distros will need to go to their respective app stores, or use the Flatpak CLI tool, to download Protontricks.

If you don't want to read text, find a video documenting the process [here](https://youtu.be/CYKCOBaZpBU?t=110)
. Thanks to Connor for providing the screenshots and transcription!

To configure the necessary parameters, you can use the app Protontricks, which you can download through the built-in app browser "Discover":

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-3335590eb3eb8be8917dab42007ea5e5c5718f99%252Flinux_modding_proton_1.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=257122ef&sv=2)

Protontricks as Shown in Discover

1.  Open Protontricks (you can search for this by selecting start menu)
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-999f960d2df3f32c350c4b6f75fdccc8f5300e08%252Flinux_modding_protontricks_2.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=739db12e&sv=2)

Opening Protontricks

1.  When Protontrick opens, select Cyberpunk:
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-764cb539ac5e67026edf1cd5efbecbda3f757b21%252Flinux_modding_3.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e7cec129&sv=2)

Finding Cyberpunk 2077 in Protontricks

1.  Choose "Select the default wineprefix":
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-5f85309166681b5e31222e502e8335047e9108c4%252Flinux_modding_4.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=447db62&sv=2)

1.  Add an override: Check **both** of the checkboxes below
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-dcd3f7fab3e05a82647dbd63b6a04ad28560cb4e%252Fprotontricks_dll_1.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a811c41f&sv=2)

Selecting `d3dcompiler_47`

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-0274baad8d09f28a51cb705f3954ab30034b880f%252Fprotontricks_dll_3.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c523b48&sv=2)

If you do not see `vcrun2022` please verify you're on the latest version of Protontricks

Apply via "OK". You may receive an error below or similar. If you do, click "Yes" to continue the installation.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-c1c55742becd57eae39fe0963b3f3592239848e3%252Fvc_run.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=52d6acd4&sv=2)

### Lutris: setting up[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#lutris-setting-up)

**Use Lutris only if you cannot make Protontricks work!**

Taken from [CET's github](https://github.com/maximegmd/CyberEngineTweaks/issues/821)

1.  Open Lutris
    
2.  Right click on the game > `Settings` > `Runner options`
    
3.  While newer versions of Lutris can use Proton, it is advised you use Wine-GE instead. Make sure you're using at least `lutris-GE-Proton8-1`
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-613bac1a1f856e399ff33fecdb32171ccb9e4840%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=30fd0888&sv=2)

1.  Scroll down to **DLL overrides**, click on **Add** to add a key. Add the following keys:
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-8dfecdd45b2ac8644e765ec95544c3604903f0a8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=399e625c&sv=2)

Launch Options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#launch-options)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You need to pass a few parameters to the Cyberpunk.exe to enable mods. Read the section for your client to find out how.

If you have typos or forget characters in your launch parameters, **they will not work**. You can copy-paste the text from the guide below.

For a full list of launch options, check [Launch parameters](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory/launch-parameters)
. Use them at own risk!

### Steam: Launch Options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#steam-launch-options)

Switch to steam and make sure that the launch options are set as below:

The command is case sensitive. Copy-paste the text from the box above to make sure.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-fb6854db363e711f715055f1c97f5489d8321029%252Flinux_modding_8.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=76760a86&sv=2)

Setting Launch Options in Steam

If this still doesn't work, check the [Cyber Engine Tweaks wiki](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyber-engine-tweaks/getting-started/installing)
 for additional instructions, or find us on [Discord](https://discord.gg/redmodding)
 in `#mod-troubleshooting`.

### GOG: Launch Options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#gog-launch-options)

You can find the launch options for GOG in the game's config menu under `Manage Installation` -> `Configure...`

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-3e0ffdc8b7901384d37847e08af073bcae849e9b%252Fgog_set_launch_args.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=291faaa3&sv=2)

As under Steam, you have to add the following:

The command is case sensitive. Copy-paste the text from the box above to make sure.

### Heroic: Launch Options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#heroic-launch-options)

While at your library right click Cyberpunk then navigate to `Settings` -> `ADVANCED` and scroll down to `Environment Variables`

Now set a new variable name `WINEDLLOVERRIDES` with a value of `winmm,version=n,b` without any quotation marks.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-22dda49822a3f60be227a4a50f1f7223445a0593%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=82ae863f&sv=2)

Don't forget to click the + button for the changes to save.

Variable Name and Value are both case sensitive, Copy-paste for certainty.

Anything that includes `**=**` and doesn't start with `**-**` goes to Environment Variables, while things starting with\*\*`-`**usually go under Game Arguments, for example**`-skipStartScreen`\*\*

Troubleshooting[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#troubleshooting)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### I can't install vcredist![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#i-cant-install-vcredist)

Try running the following shell command:

### This is not working![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#this-is-not-working)

#### Check the launch options[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#check-the-launch-options)

This is the most common error. You wouldn't believe how often people show up in the support discord channel, yell in frustration for 10 minutes, and then found out that they included an extra space, forgot a letter, or made a simple typo.

Do not be these people. Copy-paste from the guide and delete any extra spaces.

#### “Invalid file magic number”[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#invalid-file-magic-number)

Delete/uninstall the current protontrick version. Try the beta-version from here and running the commands:

[https://monroeworld.com/steamdeck/proton.txtmonroeworld.com](https://monroeworld.com/steamdeck/proton.txt)

#### Update protontricks[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#update-protontricks)

Make sure to be on the last version of protontricks. If that does not work, try the **second-to-last** version — sometimes, the tools themselves break.

You can check protontricks' [github page](https://github.com/Matoking/protontricks/issues)
. If there is anything seriously broken, you will find something there.

#### Ask for help[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/modding-on-linux#ask-for-help)

You can find us on the [modding discord](https://discord.gg/redmodding)
 in `#cet-troubleshooting` or `#red4ext-support`. Beware: If it turns out to be a typo in your launch options, there will be snark. ;)

# 3d Modelling | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling.md)
.

Check the sub-pages for more detes, or check the section's sub-pages.

Relevant guides[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling#relevant-guides)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Due to its complexity, we don't have a dedicated guide on Blender. However, if you have no idea how to start, various other guides cover part of the process:

*   [Sculpting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling/mesh-sculpting-techniques)
    
*   [Proportional editing / Refitting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/items-equipment/recolours-and-refits/r-and-r-refitting-step-by-step)
    
*   [Texture painting](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/custom-multilayermasks)
     and [normal map creation](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/self-made-normal-maps)
    
*   [Adding submeshes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling/3d-editing-submeshes)
     or [Splitting meshes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/textured-items-and-cyberpunk-materials#step-2-processing-the-downloaded-mesh)
    

For more hands-on advice, see the guide section, for example

*   [Wolvenkit Blender IO Suite](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit-blender-io-suite)
    : Importing/exporting [Meshes](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit-blender-io-suite/wkit-blender-plugin-import-export#meshes)
    
*   [Material assignment examples](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/textures-and-luts/textured-items-and-cyberpunk-materials)
    
*   [Troubleshooting your mesh edits](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling/troubleshooting-your-mesh-edits)
    
*   [Garment Support: How does it work?](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling/garment-support-how-does-it-work)
     or [Game Components explained](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do)
    

Relevant files[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling#relevant-files)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Mesh[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling#mesh)

A mesh defines the shape and surface of any in-game object.

For more information on .mesh files and how they work, see [https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/file-formats/3d-objects-.mesh-files](https://github.com/CDPR-Modding-Documentation/Cyberpunk-Modding-Docs/blob/main/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/file-formats/3d-objects-.mesh-files)

For more informations on **materials** and mesh appearances, see [Textures, Materials and Shaders](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/materials)

### Morphtarget[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling#morphtarget)

Morphtargets are a special kind of mesh used for alternate and additional shapes beyond the static shape a `.mesh` can define. If your source is a `.morphtarget`, you need to primarily work with that rather than the `.mesh`.

Read more on the [Morphtargets](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/3d-modelling/morphtargets)
 page

[PreviousOccluders and light blocking](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/files-and-what-they-do/occluders-light-and-light-blocking)

# WolvenKit | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

![Page cover](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-689541545be27ad17c010855ae03693ad5b160e4%252Fassets_-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r_-MfU4nOqozQF9jbfQej2_-MfUDMbz0Yr2sAxCjfHv_wkit_sammy_text_doublestack%2520%281600x400%29.webp%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=1248&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e0523e89&sv=2)

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit.md)
.

Summary: What is Wolvenkit?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit#summary-what-is-wolvenkit)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Originally released as W3Edit by [Sarcen](https://forums.cdprojektred.com/index.php?members/sarcen.3823905/)
, WolvenKit is the open-source (GPLv3) windows/Linux GUI editor that is the workhorse for beginner and advanced modders. It supports any game using redENGINE (v3 or v4).

As of 2026, that means **Cyberpunk 2077** and **The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt**.

![WolvenKit Screenshot March 2026](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252FVtrfFHg3jmlT0SBni2Qb%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D299bc655-d124-4e61-bf8c-8b332f8d0b1a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8530813e&sv=2)

WolvenKit Screenshot, March 2026

### Wait, this is not what I want![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit#wait-this-is-not-what-i-want)

WolvenKit has its own wiki in our space, you can get there by clicking on the link below:

[![Logo](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F4106170459-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MP_ozZVx2gRZUPXkd4r%252Favatar-1624211910487.png%3Fgeneration%3D1624211910728029%26alt%3Dmedia&width=48&height=48&sign=191c4223&sv=2)Home | WolvenKitwiki.redmodding.org](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/readme)

It contains useful info, such as

*   The[Getting Started](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/download)
    guide and an "Explain it like I'm 5" walkthrough on how to install it
    
*   Documentation explaining [Wolvenkit's Interface](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor)
    
*   A list of [known Linux issues and their workarounds](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/download#known-linux-issues-see-bug-reports-for-reporting)
    

Download and Install[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit#download-and-install)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can find a more detailed walkthrough on the WKit wiki:[Full install walkthrough (ELI5)](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/eli5-getting-started)

To get started with WolvenKit, you will need to [download](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/getting-started/download)
 the standalone application. Extract it to a folder on your harddrive (NOT [The Cyberpunk 2077 Game Directory](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory)
), for example `C:\CyberpunkModding\WolvenKit`, and start it by double-clicking the .exe file.

What can WolvenKit do for you?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit#what-can-wolvenkit-do-for-you)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To make any persistent changes, you need to work inside [WolvenKit Projects](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/wolvenkit-projects)
. Without one, you can still browse through game files, but changes won't be persistent.

Asset Management

In the [Asset Browser](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/asset-browser)
, you can look through all files in the game — no extra steps needed. By toggling the Mod Browser button, you can see modded intead of base game files.

Mod Packing

With just the [push of a toolbar button](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/menu#install-and-launch-game)
, you can load your mod directly into your game ([even while it's running](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/redhottools/rht-hot-reload)
), or pack it for upload on Nexus.

File Editing

The built-in [File Editor](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/editor/file-editor)
 lets you edit any REDengine4 files, including in-place buffer data.

Import/Export

Easy [Import/Export](https://wiki.redmodding.org/wolvenkit/wolvenkit-app/usage/import-export)
 of REDEngine4 files (for example: `.xbm` =>`.png`, `.mesh` => `.glb`) for editing in external software like [Blender](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit-blender-io-suite)
.

World Editing

Wolvenkit is well-integrated with the [World Editing](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/world-editing)
toolchain - you can convert your temporary world edit to just another part of Night City.

Quest Editing

Our quest editor is not as comfortable as CDPR's (we hope!!!), but you can [create your own quests](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/modding-guides/quest)
.

[PreviousModding Tools](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools)
[NextWolvenkit Blender IO Suite](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-creators-theory/modding-tools/wolvenkit-blender-io-suite)

Last updated 4 months ago

---

# The Cyberpunk 2077 Game Directory | Cyberpunk 2077 Modding

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory.md)
.

Summary[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#summary)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Published:** Jan 27 2024 by [mana vortex](mailto:undefined)
 **Last documented update:** January 19th, 2026 by [minnierylands](mailto:undefined)

* * *

This page will tell you

*   What your Cyberpunk 2077 game directory is (with extra information in the expandable box)
    
*   How you can find the game folder
    
*   How to use Windows Search to delete leftover files from a mod
    

* * *

What is the game directory?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#what-is-the-game-directory)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The game directory is called `Cyberpunk 2077` (unless you re-named it). It's where Windows puts everything it needs to run the game when you click the icon — including mods.

What's the difference between folders and files?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#whats-the-difference-between-folders-and-files)

_Most of us Netrunners have grown up with this and are using it every day for literal decades, so this kind of knowledge is considered pretty basic — but if you don't have it, don't feel bad! I won't explain everything in detail here, but there's a great guide on_ [_uis.georgetown.edu_](https://uis.georgetown.edu/file-explorer/)
 _on how to use the Windows File Explorer._

A **folder** (also called **directory**) has a yellow icon: 📁 It's a container for **files** and other folders.

A **file** can be anything and have any kind of icon. They don't usually contain other files or folders, although exceptions exist.

You browse through this using the Windows Explorer.

Click on the picture below to zoom in!

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-fcdadfe1c222093a6585df2667a42ed1b9a671be%252Fcyberpunk_dir_windows_explorer.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9f6cf861&sv=2)

* * *

How can I find the game folder?[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#how-can-i-find-the-game-folder)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Unless you changed the default settings, your game is installed here:

Steam

`C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Cyberpunk 2077`

GOG

`C:\Program Files (x86)\GOG Galaxy\Games\Cyberpunk 2077`

Epic

`C:\Program Files\Epic Games\Cyberpunk 2077`

Opening a Windows Explorer directly to your game folder[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#opening-a-windows-explorer-directly-to-your-game-folder)

You can open a Windows Explorer in the game folder like this:

1.  Press `Windows+R` to open the `Run` dialogue
    
2.  type `explorer` (don't forget the space)
    
3.  Copy and paste the game path from the table above into the text box
    
4.  Press `Enter` or click `OK`
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-02e0313c9994b0b0755fb0cf6da1191978a12cf5%252Fgame_folder_open_explorer.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=975f8f0&sv=2)

### It's not there![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#its-not-there)

If you don't know your game directory, you can find it through your game launcher:

Steam

GOG

Epic Games

Right-click on the game in your library, then select `Manage` -> `Browse local files`

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-6e86b8cd6ca4805901554845ba471d7ea1d8451b%252Fcyberpunk_game_directory_steam.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=22a60043&sv=2)

With the game selected in your library, click on the `More` button, then on `Manage Installation` and `Show folder…`

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-885f383c7333467710e496b290be04f58166b5e6%252Fcyberpunk_dir_gog_show_files.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b0204e6d&sv=2)

Right-click on your game in the list and select `Manage`. Then, click on the folder icon:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-abc3027dd4bd69d8d2277a0e1115c2eb406f62ff%252Fcyberpunk_game_directory_epic.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8676b628&sv=2)

* * *

Copying the folder path[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#copying-the-folder-path)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  Select the folder path by clicking on the empty bit to the right of the explorer's path bar:
    

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-81b0df5fe338de2cb00b6dc473153ff0a925b5a5%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a3bb3f81&sv=2)

1.  The entire path will be highlighted (if it's not, you can do that by pressing `Ctrl+A`)
    
2.  Press `Ctrl+C` to copy it to your clipboard
    

* * *

Deleting residual files with Windows Search[](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/for-mod-users/users-modding-cyberpunk-2077/the-cyberpunk-2077-game-directory#deleting-residual-files-with-windows-search)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Once the Cyberpunk folder is open in your Windows Explorer, you can use the `Windows Search bar` in the upper right corner (Hotkey: `Ctrl+F`) to find files by name.

For example, if you have trouble with atelier and have decided to do a clean reinstall, you can search for `atelier`:

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-9a64860b71e8ea2c51049297bd40f24ca739316a%252Fusing_windows_search.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=87b9b05b&sv=2)

This will also find most of your atelier stores. If you use Vortex, that's no problem - otherwise, you may want to backup before you delete.

You're seeing the atelier install folder (good) and a bunch of atelier stores (bad, these are innocent). You can either double-click on the `virtual-atelier-full` folder (first item) to navigate there, or use either of the following options.

Finding the Folder Path in the Search Results

Deleting From the Search Results

**Finding the folder path in the search results**

If you right-click on a folder or file, you will see a menu entry "`open folder/file location`".

This will open a new Windows Explorer window in the folder **containing** the item you selected, letting you browse and delete.

![](https://wiki.redmodding.org/cyberpunk-2077-modding/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1427525421-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F4gzcGtLrr90pVjAWVdTc%252Fuploads%252Fgit-blob-070cd081b1bad3a3fc3ffcd91e3fe0c0825c57b4%252Fwindows_search_open_folder.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d6d5f6fc&sv=2)

**Deleting from the search results**

Select multiple items from the list by holding the `Ctrl` key while you click.

Once you have selected everything that you want to delete, simply press the `delete` key, or right-click and select `Delete`.
